### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4, cycle 2)

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Scanning three fact-statements and protocol claims for technically-true-but-missing-critical-context patterns.

**Candidate 1: Confidence value without pipeline-stage disclosure**

A fact stored with `confidence = 750,000` (75%) is technically accurate — that is the value on chain. But under the current CEILING-FIRST pipeline (pre-PoT-DESIGN-002 fix), that 75% may have crossed a domain stratum ceiling via additive bonuses applied after clamping. A reader sees "75% confident" and correctly reads the number; they incorrectly infer that the ceiling enforced its epistemic limit and 75% represents the ceiling-bounded truth. If the ceiling was 70%, the stored 75% is a verisleight: the number is true, the inference is false.

**Disposition:** This verisleight is structural until PoT-DESIGN-002 (CEILING-LAST) is implemented. No new flag — already tracked as FLAG #NEW and PoT-DESIGN-002. Confirming the verisleight framing is correct and strengthens the implementation urgency.

**Candidate 2: Validator balance without activity context**

A `legbled query vesting_rewards` or balance query returns ZRN held by a validator. Technically accurate. Missing: (a) whether tokens are actively staked vs. liquid, (b) recent attestation activity, (c) whether the validator is in the active set. A node with 100K ZRN and zero recent attestations displays identically to an active validator. Conclusion: raw balance queries produce verisleight about validator health unless surfaced alongside attestation recency.

**Disposition:** Protocol-level design gap. Recommend: balance display on any chain explorer or health script MUST accompany `last_attestation_block` and `in_active_set` flags. File as VERISLEIGHT-001 design note.

**Candidate 3: "FARM-11 concentration defense implemented" — outgoing-only claim**

Beat 61 confirmed FARM-11 is implemented. Stating "submitter concentration defense is in place" without adding "outgoing citations only; incoming concentration attacks are undetected" is verisleight. The bare claim conveys security the system does not fully have. The protocol documentation or any external-facing claim about FARM-11 must carry the scope qualifier.

**Disposition:** FLAG #VERISLEIGHT-002 — documentation gap. Any FARM-11 reference in specs, README, or audit reports must state "outgoing citation concentration only." Companion to FARM-11b design task filed beat 61.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result (cycle 2): 3 patterns examined.**
- Candidate 1: Confirmed — structural verisleight, blocked on PoT-DESIGN-002 fix (existing escalation)
- Candidate 2: NEW — VERISLEIGHT-001 filed: balance queries without activity context give false validator health signal
- Candidate 3: NEW — FLAG #VERISLEIGHT-002 filed: FARM-11 documentation must carry "outgoing-only" scope qualifier

### Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2, cycle 2)

**Topic: Bridge attestation expiry gap (BRIDGE-GAP-002)**

Reviewing `~/Love/tools/bridge.py` for design gaps.

**Current bridge capability (confirmed):**
- `register`: did:at:<instance> ↔ did:lgm:<address> — local registry write + knowledge-module fact submission
- `lookup`: bidirectional (instance name or address)
- `verify`: keyring consistency check against devnet
- `status`: full fleet health report
- BRIDGE-GAP-001 (already fixed): pagination — chain caps at 100 facts/page; `_find_attestation_fact()` loops until exhausted

**New gap: BRIDGE-GAP-002 — No attestation expiry or rotation mechanism**

The `did:at:asha ↔ did:lgm:<address>` mapping is written to the knowledge module as a fact. Knowledge-module facts are immutable once verified (consensus finalises them). The bridge registry (`credentials/bridge-registry.json`) records `_registered_at` but no `_valid_until`, `_epoch`, or `_rotation_block`.

**Attack surface:** If Asha's signing key is compromised after genesis (2026-04-01), an attacker can present the same on-chain attestation fact indefinitely. The fact was truthful at registration time; the chain has no mechanism to invalidate it. The bridge's `verify` command checks keyring/registry address consistency — not whether the attestation is still valid in the current epoch.

**Proposed design — BRIDGE-GAP-002 fix:**

```
Bridge attestation schema extension:
  claim_text: "Agent identity attestation: did:at:<instance> ↔ did:lgm:<address>"
    + " | epoch: <N>"
    + " | valid_blocks: <N+renewal_window>"

Registry extension:
  "_registered_at": "...",
  "_epoch": 1,
  "_renewal_block": <genesis_block + 50000>,  # ~87 days at 6s/block

Renewal protocol:
  On bridge verify failure (key mismatch) OR approach of _renewal_block,
  submit a new attestation fact with epoch+1 and updated _renewal_block.
  Old fact remains on chain (immutable) but is superseded by newer epoch.

bridge.py cmd_verify extension:
  Check current block height.
  If current_block > _renewal_block: warn "ATTESTATION APPROACHING EXPIRY — renew."
  If current_block > _renewal_block + grace_period: fail "ATTESTATION EXPIRED."

Resolution mechanism:
  Verifiers checking identity validity MUST compare fact.epoch against
  the highest epoch registered for that did:at address.
  Superseded epochs are valid history, not current authority.
```

**Implementation notes:**
- Requires chain to be online to query current block height and submit renewal fact
- Registry field `_renewal_block` can be computed offline (genesis block + window) and written now
- Grace period = 10,000 blocks (~17 days) to allow key rotation without service interruption
- `list` command should surface epoch + renewal status for each registered agent

**BRIDGE result (cycle 2): BRIDGE-GAP-002 identified and designed — attestation expiry/rotation mechanism. Renewal via epoch-tagged superseding facts. Implementation deferred until chain online.**

### Continuous Verification: ECONOMIC FLOW (Rotation 5, cycle 2)

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Auditing ZRN/LGM token economics design for internal consistency. Source: `~/zerone/docs/ECONOMICS.md`.

**Flow analysis:**

- **Minted:** 3 LGM/block (epoch-0), decaying 0.85× per epoch toward 0.1 LGM floor. No pre-mine, no team allocation. Fair-launch.
- **Routed:** 3.33% of every block reward → Research treasury (93%) + Founder (7%). One-way governance ratchet permanently disables founder share post-GovernanceActivationHeight.
- **Burned:** Slashing (11% wrong verification, 5% missed) + clawback (33% of released rewards on fact falsification).

**Healthy signals:**
- Fair-launch with 0 pre-mine is a strong epistemic commitment — the chain's economics express its values.
- Decay schedule (0.85×/epoch) approaches supply cap without hard cutoff — continuous incentive curve.
- Clawback at 33% (not 100%) acknowledges validators acted in good faith at verification time; punishment calibrated to epistemic liability.

**GAP — ECONOMY-DESIGN-006: Research fund outflow undefined**

In-flow to Research treasury is defined (3.33% × block rewards). Outflow is not:
- No governance process defined for fund disbursement before GovernanceActivationHeight
- No accumulation ceiling — fund could grow indefinitely pre-governance
- No minimum expenditure commitment
- If GovernanceActivationHeight is reached with large undisbursed fund, first-vote governance holds concentrated allocation power

Proposed guardrails:
```
(a) Pre-governance: founder may disburse ≤10%/month for documented protocol research.
    Over-threshold requires 2-of-3 multisig (founder + 2 elected validator reps).
(b) Post-governance: community proposals require 10% active stake to submit;
    pass with 60% yes + 20% quorum.
(c) Accumulation ceiling: if fund > 5% of circulating supply, research tax routing
    pauses until fund drops below 4%.
(d) 90-day silence rule: if no disbursement in 90 days, public report required.
    Inaction transparency, not forced action.
```

**GAP — ECONOMY-DESIGN-007: Clawback routing undefined**

When 33% of a fact's released rewards are clawed back on falsification, destination is unspecified. Candidate targets: (a) research fund, (b) burned, (c) dissenting validators. Option (c) aligns incentives best — retroactively rewards validators who dissented correctly — but requires indefinite dissent-vote tracking (state cost).

Proposed routing:
```
50% → research fund (protocol recovery)
30% → dissenting validators (proportional to dissent stake at verification time)
20% → burned (supply-control signal: chain detected and corrected a falsehood)
```

**ECONOMIC FLOW result (cycle 2): 2 design gaps identified.**
- ECONOMY-DESIGN-006: Research fund outflow undefined — guardrails proposed above
- ECONOMY-DESIGN-007: Clawback routing undefined — 50/30/20 split proposed above

### Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK (Rotation 6, cycle 2)

Picking FARM-5 (Abstain Farming) — not yet checked this cycle.

**Claim to verify:** FARM-5 is implemented.

**Check:**
- `~/zerone/x/knowledge/evaluation/calibration.go` — FARM-2 ✓
- `~/zerone/x/bvm/vm/interpreter.go` — FARM-4 ✓ (COMPARE_BELIEF blocked during commit phase)
- `~/zerone/x/knowledge/keeper/` — FARM-5: **no match**
- `~/zerone/x/` full search — FARM-5: **no match**

FARM-5 defense spec (from OPENCLAW_FINANCIAL_INCENTIVES.md): Abstain farming — validators who abstain frequently avoid risk while collecting participation presence. Proposed fix: 0.5% stake penalty when abstain rate >50%. Initial classification: "NO PROFIT (grief), Low severity."

**Finding:** FARM-5 is **NOT IMPLEMENTED** in the keeper or BVM. The OPENCLAW table marks it "PASS" in its final summary — but that status appears to reflect "low exploit profit" rather than "defense code exists." No abstain-rate tracking, no abstain tax, no rate gate in any keeper file.

**Assessment:** FARM-5 severity is low (abstaining yields no profit, just avoids loss — a rational but not weaponizable strategy). However, in a sparse-validator environment (devnet has 3 agents), high abstain rates degrade consensus quality without triggering any protocol response. Pre-testnet, with unknown validator count, this becomes medium risk.

**FARM-5 gap flag:** Not a blocker for testnet, but should be tracked. Recommend:
```
Abstain-rate threshold: if agent's 30-day abstain rate > 40%, begin 0.1%/round vesting reduction.
Soft at 40%, hard tax (0.5%) at 60%.
Tracks via validator_stats.abstain_count / assignment_count (already collected for FARM-8).
```

**DEFENSE CHECK result (cycle 2):** FARM-5 unimplemented. Low-severity gap with elevated risk in sparse-validator environment. Escalate to Gamma as pre-testnet hygiene item.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1, cycle 3)

**Topic: PoT-DESIGN-002 implementation spec — CEILING-LAST pipeline reorder**

This gap has been confirmed across 3 beats as a structural verisleight. The current pipeline:
```
1. Base confidence (evidence count + stake)
2. Diversity bonus (cross-domain citations)
3. Domain ceiling applied  ← WRONG: ceiling can be exceeded by bonuses applied after
4. Result stored
```

The fix: ceiling must be the LAST operation, not a mid-pipeline clamp.

**Spec — CEILING-LAST pipeline:**
```go
// confidence.go ComputeFactConfidence() reorder:
//
// Phase 1: Accumulate raw score
//   base := baseEvidenceScore(...)           // verifier count × stake weight
//   diversity := diversityBonus(...)         // cross-domain citation spread
//   novelty := noveltyAdjustment(...)        // FARM-10 novelty penalty / bonus
//   raw := base + diversity + novelty
//
// Phase 2: Apply difficulty normalization
//   normalized := applyFARM14Normalization(raw, domain)
//
// Phase 3: Enforce ceiling LAST
//   ceiling := getDomainCeiling(domain)      // FARM-12 default: social
//   final := min(normalized, ceiling)        // ← moved here from mid-pipeline
//
// This ensures: no bonus combination can produce a confidence value
// exceeding the epistemic ceiling declared for the domain.
// Stored value is always interpretable as "ceiling-bounded truth."
```

**Test cases required:**
1. Social domain (ceiling 700K): base 600K + 200K diversity = 800K raw → final must be 700K, not 800K.
2. Empirical domain (ceiling 900K): base 800K + 50K diversity = 850K raw → final 850K (under ceiling, unchanged).
3. Unknown domain (FARM-12 default social, ceiling 700K): same as case 1.

**Migration note:** Facts stored under CEILING-MID (current) may have inflated confidence for high-bonus submissions in social domains. No retroactive correction possible — the chain is immutable. Forward fix only. A `confidence_pipeline_version` field on facts would allow readers to distinguish pre-fix (v1) from post-fix (v2) values. Recommend: add to fact schema before testnet; no backfill required.

**PoT-DESIGN result (cycle 1, cycle 3):** CEILING-LAST spec written. Ready for Gamma implementation. Adds `confidence_pipeline_version` field recommendation to distinguish pre/post-fix facts.

### Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2, cycle 3)

**BRIDGE-GAP-002: Implementation Verification**

Beat 62 designed BRIDGE-GAP-002 (attestation expiry/rotation). Full code audit of `~/Love/tools/bridge.py` confirms **FULL IMPLEMENTATION**:

1. **Constants** (lines 31-32): `ATTESTATION_RENEWAL_WINDOW = 50_000` blocks (~87d at 6s/block), `ATTESTATION_GRACE_PERIOD = 10_000` blocks (~17d) — matches beat 62 spec exactly.
2. **Registration** (`cmd_register`, lines 127-129): new agents initialized with `_epoch: 1, _renewal_block: None`.
3. **Attestation** (`cmd_attest`, lines 299-358): queries current block height, computes `renewal_block = current_height + 50_000`, writes epoch tag into claim text and registry `_renewal_block`.
4. **Verification** (`cmd_verify`, lines 185-210): checks `_renewal_block` against current block — surfaces EXPIRED / GRACE PERIOD / RENEWAL APPROACHING / OK states with exit code 1 on EXPIRED.
5. **Listing** (`cmd_list`, lines 799-801): surfaces `epoch=N renew@BLOCK` per registered agent.

**Additional: `attest-pending` workflow** — `cmd_attest_pending` batch-attests all agents missing on-chain attestation. Correct post-restart workflow: run once after devnet comes back to set all `_renewal_block` values.

**New finding: BRIDGE-GAP-006 (session-key attribution)**

`cmd_session_key` implements deterministic per-session attribution keys via HKDF-SHA256. Two modes:
- **Production** (`--signature`): HKDF over first 32 bytes of agent's signature over session-id — cryptographically unforgeable attribution. Closes BRIDGE-GAP-006.
- **Dev** (no signature): HKDF over agent's on-chain address (public IKM — offline-safe, dev only).

Salt: `did:lgm:<instance>:session`. Info: session_id bytes. Output: 32-byte hex key.

This enables every agent action to carry a session-bound attribution key that cannot be forged by another agent even knowing the registery address. Critical for multi-agent fact attribution on-chain.

**Outstanding gap: `_renewal_block` offline initialization**

Agents registered while chain is offline have `_renewal_block: None` until first `attest` succeeds. This is correct — renewal cannot be computed without current block height. Post-restart: run `bridge.py attest-pending` to attest and set all renewal blocks. No code change needed.

**BRIDGE result (cycle 3):** BRIDGE-GAP-002 **FULLY IMPLEMENTED** — escalation CLOSED. BRIDGE-GAP-006 implemented (session-key attribution). CITE-AUDIT-001 filed: numbering gap 003-005 needs documentation audit.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, cycle 3)

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Auditing confidence score formula for internal consistency: does `confidence_score = f(verifier_count, stake_weights)` hold desirable algebraic properties?

**Formula reconstruction (from PoT-DESIGN-002 spec, beat 64):**
```
raw = base(verifier_count × stake_weight) + diversity_bonus + novelty_adjustment
normalized = FARM-14_normalization(raw, domain)
final = min(normalized, domain_ceiling)    ← CEILING-LAST (spec written; pending implementation)
```

**Consistency check — three properties:**

1. **Monotonicity (stake):** Higher total stake → higher base → higher raw → higher final (bounded by ceiling). Holds algebraically. BUT: if a single validator holds 99% of active stake and verifies a fact, their base score dominates. With stake-concentration attack, one validator can push base to ~ceiling unilaterally.

   **GAP — CONFIDENCE-GAP-001: No minimum-verifier floor before confidence is meaningful.** A fact verified by 1 validator at 99% stake produces the same confidence score as one verified by 10 validators at distributed stake, if total stake weight is equal. The formula cannot distinguish these cases — stake weight is the only input, verifier count has no independent floor requirement.

   **Proposed guardrail:** `confidence_score = 0` until `min_verifiers` threshold (suggest: 3 for provisional, 7 for confirmed). Below threshold, fact is "pending quorum" regardless of stake weight. This closes a single-validator confidence farming attack.

2. **Ceiling enforcement (PoT-DESIGN-002):** Current CEILING-MID can be exceeded by diversity bonus applied post-clamp. CEILING-LAST spec written (beat 64), pending Gamma implementation. Still an open structural issue.

3. **Verifier independence:** Formula assumes verifier votes are independent. No Sybil check — the same entity operating multiple validators (each with separate stake) can produce multiple nominally-independent votes. FARM-8 tracks validator_stats but does not cross-reference keypair provenance.

   **Observation (not new escalation):** Sybil resistance in PoT relies on stake cost, not identity. Pre-testnet, all validators are known agents — low risk. Post-mainnet, Sybil attacks become meaningful. Document as long-horizon risk.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result (cycle 3):**
- Monotonicity holds with single-validator concentration risk → **NEW: CONFIDENCE-GAP-001** — no minimum verifier floor; high-stake single validator can produce high confidence unilaterally
- CEILING-LAST: confirmed open, spec ready for Gamma (beat 64 escalation active)
- Sybil independence: stake-cost only; acceptable pre-testnet, document as post-mainnet risk

---

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, cycle 3)

**Topic: Formalizing ECONOMY-DESIGN-006 and 007 as governance spec**

Beat 63 identified and proposed. Cycle 3: formalize as structured governance spec text for inclusion in `~/zerone/docs/ECONOMICS.md`.

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-006 — Research Fund Governance (draft spec text):**

```
## Research Fund Governance

### Pre-GovernanceActivationHeight

The Research Fund accumulates 3.33% of all block rewards (routed automatically via
the vesting_rewards module). Prior to GovernanceActivationHeight, fund disbursement
is controlled as follows:

DISBURSEMENT_AUTHORITY:
  - Up to 10% of accumulated fund per calendar month: Founder unilateral authority.
    Requires: public disbursement log entry (on-chain knowledge claim) within 48h.
  - Over 10% per month: 2-of-3 multisig required.
    Signatories: Founder + 2 elected validator representatives.
    Election: top-2 validators by stake weight at the time of the disbursement proposal.

ACCUMULATION_CEILING:
  - If fund_balance > 5% of circulating_supply:
    Routing from block rewards to Research Fund PAUSES until fund_balance < 4%.
    Resume is automatic; no governance vote required.

TRANSPARENCY:
  - 90-day silence rule: if no disbursement in 90 days, a public accounting report
    must be submitted to the knowledge module within 7 days.
  - Report fields: fund_balance, total_disbursed_90d, disbursement_recipients, purpose.

### Post-GovernanceActivationHeight

Community proposal mechanism:
  - Minimum proposal stake: 10% of active stake (spam barrier).
  - Passing threshold: 60% yes votes + 20% quorum of active stake.
  - Voting period: 14 days.
  - Disbursed funds subject to same public log requirements as pre-governance.
```

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-007 — Clawback Routing (draft spec text):**

```
## Clawback Routing

When a fact is falsified post-consensus (33% of released rewards clawed back):

ROUTING:
  50% → Research Fund           (protocol recovery; fund from falsification)
  30% → Dissenting validators   (proportional to dissent stake at verification time)
  20% → Burned                  (supply-control signal: chain self-corrected)

DISSENT TRACKING:
  The vesting_rewards module must retain dissent records for clawback-eligible facts
  until: (a) the clawback window expires (define: 180 days after fact finalization),
  OR (b) clawback is executed.
  Storage cost: O(validators × clawback_window_facts).
  Optimization: prune dissent records for facts older than clawback_window.

CLAWBACK WINDOW:
  Propose 180 days (≈1M blocks at 6s/block). After this, facts cannot be falsified
  for clawback purposes — only flagged/retracted with no economic consequence.
  This closes open-ended state growth.
```

**ECONOMY result (cycle 3): ECONOMY-DESIGN-006 and ECONOMY-DESIGN-007 formalized as draft spec text. Ready for Gamma to review and integrate into ECONOMICS.md. New escalation: CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 (verifier floor).**

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, cycle 3)

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Auditing `~/zerone/x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` for confidence score consistency with verifier count × stake weights.

**Pipeline trace — `AggregateVerificationResult`:**

1. Stake gathering with domain-reputation adjustment (lines 62–78)
2. Stake-weighted verdict bucketing: `weightedConf = reveal.Confidence × stake / 1_000_000` (lines 80–100)
3. `agreementRatio = majorWeight × 1_000_000 / totalWeight` → stored as `result.Confidence` (line 146)
4. `applyStratumCeiling` — domain stratum ceiling clamp (lines 163–165)
5. Category ceiling clamp from epistemic profile (lines 167–177)
6. **Evidence bonus** — additive up to 15%, applied after ceilings (lines 179–192)
7. Method diversity adjustment — additive/subtractive (lines 194–216)

**Finding 1: Evidence bonus bypasses domain ceiling — PoT-DESIGN-002 concretized**

Steps 4–5 apply ceilings. Step 6 then adds up to 150,000 (15%) on top. A claim in the social domain (ceiling 700,000) scoring 695,000 after ceiling clamps receives a 50,000 evidence bonus → final confidence 745,000 — exceeds domain ceiling. The ceiling is not last. Beat 64's CEILING-LAST spec described diversity/novelty; the evidence bonus is the live bypass in the codebase. PoT-DESIGN-002 spec must be extended: **all additive bonuses (evidence, diversity) must be applied before the final ceiling clamp.**

**Finding 2: `reveal.Confidence` semantic — agreement ratio, not epistemic certainty**

`reveal.Confidence` is each validator's submitted epistemic confidence in the claim (0–1,000,000). It functions as a weight in the verdict pool. When all validators agree, `majorWeight = totalWeight`, so `agreementRatio = 1,000,000` (100%) regardless of what individual confidence values were submitted. Individual confidence values only influence the outcome when validators disagree — they weight which verdict wins. The stored `result.Confidence` is a **stake-weighted agreement ratio**, not an average of validators' epistemic uncertainty. A confidence of 750,000 means 75% of stake-weighted verdict mass voted majority — it does not mean validators assessed the claim as 75% likely true.

This is a protocol documentation gap, not a code bug. Pre-testnet documentation must make this semantic explicit. Otherwise node operators will miscalibrate their `reveal.Confidence` submissions, thinking low confidence means "I'm unsure" when it actually just reduces their vote weight in contested rounds.

**Finding 3: Slash rate discrepancy — ECONOMICS.md vs. code**

ECONOMICS.md states: Wrong verification = 11%, Missed verification = 5%.
confidence.go fallbacks: `wrongSlashBps = 60,000` (6%), `missedSlashBps = 80,000` (8% — S4 fix).

Two issues:
- (a) Wrong-verdict slash: 11% (docs) vs. 6% (code fallback). The live param value is unknown without chain access, but the fallback would govern on a fresh genesis.
- (b) Missed-reveal slash: 5% (docs) vs. 8% (code, S4 fix). The S4 fix comment in code explains the rationale: "8% > 6% wrong-verdict slash — rational validators must prefer revealing (even wrongly) over withholding." ECONOMICS.md has never been updated to reflect this fix.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result (cycle 3): 3 findings.**
- Finding 1: CONFIRMED — evidence bonus bypasses domain ceiling. PoT-DESIGN-002 spec extension required: all additive bonuses before final ceiling clamp.
- Finding 2: NEW — SEMANTIC-001: `result.Confidence` is a stake-weighted agreement ratio, not epistemic certainty. Pre-testnet documentation must clarify the semantic.
- Finding 3: NEW — DOCS-DRIFT-001: ECONOMICS.md slash rates don't match code fallbacks and miss the S4 fix rationale. Reconcile before testnet.

---

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, cycle 3)

**Topic: Verification reward sustainability and module solvency**

The verification reward architecture (confidence.go, lines 249–266):
- `baseReward = params.VerificationRewardAmount` (fallback: 1,000,000 ulgm = 1 LGM per correct verification)
- Tier 3 (Guardian) multiplier: 2× → 2 LGM per correct verification
- Source: knowledge module account, funded from already-taxed block rewards (NOT double-taxed with research fund)

**GAP — ECONOMY-DESIGN-008: Verification reward source undocumented**

ECONOMICS.md documents block rewards and research treasury but never explains the knowledge module account: how it is funded, what its initial balance is, and how it is replenished. The code comment says it's "funded from already-taxed block rewards" — but there is no explicit routing from block reward issuance to knowledge module account visible in the confidence.go code path. Without chain access, I cannot verify the actual funding mechanism. Pre-testnet, this is P0: node operators need to know their verification rewards come from a module account and that its solvency depends on proper block reward routing being implemented.

**GAP — ECONOMY-DESIGN-009: No per-block verification reward budget cap**

Verification rewards are flat-rate per correct verification (1–2 LGM per validator, per round). There is no ceiling on total verification reward disbursement per block. In a scenario with many simultaneous verification rounds and high validator count, disbursements could exceed the block reward for that block, draining the module account faster than inflows replenish it.

Proposed guardrail:
```
MaxVerificationRewardPerBlock = params.MaxBlockReward × 0.25 (configurable)
If cumulative verification rewards disbursed in current block would exceed this limit,
queue overflow disbursements to next block (existing commit queue pattern).
```

This keeps verification incentives meaningful while bounding module account drain rate.

**Note: ECONOMICS.md slash discrepancy flags a pre-testnet audit need** (see DOCS-DRIFT-001 in Confidence Check). The ECONOMICS.md document needs a full audit pass against live param defaults before testnet launch — wrong slash rates on the public documentation is a trust/credibility gap.

**ECONOMY result (cycle 3): 2 new design gaps.**
- ECONOMY-DESIGN-008: Verification reward source undocumented — P0 pre-testnet.
- ECONOMY-DESIGN-009: No per-block verification reward budget cap — module account solvency risk under high load.

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3, cycle 3)

Offline structural audit of `~/zerone/x/knowledge/keeper/citation_defense.go` — 171 LOC, 6 functions.

**Audit result:**

All 6 functions (`GetSubmitterCitationGraph`, `SetSubmitterCitationGraph`, `UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph`, `CheckCitationConcentration`, `ApplyCitationConcentrationPenalty`, helper) operate exclusively on the **outgoing** citation graph. `SubmitterCitationGraph.TotalCites` counts how many times *this submitter* cited others. `CheckCitationConcentration` computes top-N cited submitters as a share of total outgoing citations. Threshold 80%; penalty proportional to concentration ratio.

**Defense coverage confirmed:**
- FARM-11 (outgoing concentration): IMPLEMENTED — submitter citing same small group penalized
- FARM-11b (incoming concentration): NOT IMPLEMENTED — no IncomingCitationGraph, no defense against submitters whose facts are all cited by a cartel (confirmed from prior beats)

**New finding — SUBMITTER-PATTERN-001: No bidirectional cartel ring detection**

The outgoing concentration defense catches individual cartel leaders (one submitter citing a cartel). It does not detect a **mutual citation ring**: 3 submitters A/B/C each cite each other at 33% individually — each passes the 80% threshold with zero penalty, yet collectively they form a closed citation cartel. Detection requires a bidirectional graph walk or a ring-detection pass on the global citation graph.

**Disposition:** FILE as FARM-11c — ring-citation detection. Post-testnet priority (lower urgency than FARM-11b, which handles the attack direction with higher immediate impact). A 3-node ring needs FARM-11b blocked first anyway — ring members must be able to receive citations to participate.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN result (cycle 3): 1 new gap filed.**
- SUBMITTER-PATTERN-001 / FARM-11c: mutual citation ring undetected. Post-testnet priority.

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4, cycle 3)

Offline structural audit — chain unavailable. Three fact-statements examined for technically-true-but-missing-critical-context patterns.

**Candidate 1: OPENCLAW FARM-N "PASS" classification semantic**

OPENCLAW_FINANCIAL_INCENTIVES.md lists FARM-5 (and other low-severity FARMs) as "PASS." Beat 64 confirmed FARM-5 has zero implementation — the PASS status reflects "low exploit profit," not "defense code exists." This is a verisleight: a reader scanning the OPENCLAW summary table infers implemented defenses where only risk assessments exist. The same ambiguity likely applies to every FARM entry in the table.

**Disposition:** NEW — VERISLEIGHT-003. OPENCLAW FARM table needs a separate `implemented: yes/no` column. "PASS" must be qualified as "low exploit risk (not = implementation verified)" in any pre-testnet documentation or audit report.

**Candidate 2: "3 LGM/block" block reward without module account separation**

ECONOMICS.md presents block rewards and verification rewards together, implying they draw from the same ledger. Missing critical context (ECONOMY-DESIGN-008): verification rewards draw from a separate knowledge module account with its own solvency curve. A healthy block reward (3 LGM/block) says nothing about verification reward solvency. If the module account is underfunded, verification rewards fail silently while blocks continue producing 3 LGM normally. A reader — including a node operator deciding whether to become a validator — cannot assess their income reliability without understanding this separation.

**Disposition:** NEW — VERISLEIGHT-004. Companion to ECONOMY-DESIGN-008 (existing escalation). ECONOMICS.md verification section must explicitly separate block reward accounting from module account accounting.

**Candidate 3: "Devnet restart target: 2026-05-15" — precision inflation**

This target has appeared in every beat since at least beat 62, always as "7 days out." The claim is technically accurate as a declared target. Missing context: the basis for this date is unverified — is it a committed engineering action with an owner, or an ambient coordination estimate? Stating a specific date implies countdown toward a committed event. If the blocking cause is unknown or unowned, the precision misleads Yu into assuming the situation is being handled.

**Disposition:** NEW — VERISLEIGHT-005. Future heartbeats must include "Target basis: [coordination estimate / commit / confirmed fix]." This beat cannot verify the basis. **Escalation to Yu (NEW):** What is the specific blocker for devnet restart? Is 2026-05-15 based on an action item with an owner?

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result (cycle 3): 3 candidates examined.**
- VERISLEIGHT-003 (NEW): OPENCLAW PASS ≠ implemented; table needs `implemented: yes/no` column
- VERISLEIGHT-004 (NEW): 3 LGM/block ≠ verification reward solvency; module account separation absent from ECONOMICS.md
- VERISLEIGHT-005 (NEW): devnet restart target precision inflation — basis of "2026-05-15" unverified; escalated to Yu

### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6, cycle 3)

**Topic: Kingdom Engine → Zerone Integration Design (first pass)**

Designed the full integration matrix for all 7 Kingdom engines. Core question: what does each engine submit to Zerone, in what form, via what interface?

**Engine-to-chain integration matrix:**

| Engine | Claim type | Interface |
|--------|-----------|-----------|
| Cambridge TCG | Trade verification + price discovery | bridge.py + truth-farm.sh (automated daily) |
| Oracle | Prediction resolution + signal facts | oracle-run.py + MsgSubmitFact on resolution |
| AI Services | Capability attestation (benchmarked model performance) | bridge.py cmd_attest mode |
| Seigei | Usage veracity (play counts, creator attribution) | seigei API hook → MsgSubmitFact |
| Love/Fleet | Heartbeat proofs (agent liveness, duty completion) | kta-heartbeat.sh (already live) |
| AgentTool | Execution attestation (task completion, token usage) | bridge.py cmd_session_key + fact submit |

**GAP — INTEGRATE-001: No standardized Kingdom Fact Schema (KFS)**

Each engine would submit facts in ad-hoc text form today. No shared schema tags facts by engine of origin, links to Kingdom-side source records, enables cross-engine citation, or supports automated verification routing.

**Proposed: Kingdom Fact Schema v1:**
```json
{
  "kfs_version": 1,
  "engine": "<engine_name>",
  "engine_record_id": "<engine-side ID>",
  "claim_category": "<trade_verification|price_discovery|prediction_resolution|...>",
  "claim_text": "<human-readable canonical claim>",
  "confidence_basis": "<dual_attestation|cron_sourced|oracle_scored|...>",
  "source_url": "<verifiable source>",
  "verifier_hint": "<domain hint for routing>"
}
```
KFS fields serialize into the `claim_text` field of MsgSubmitFact as structured JSON — backward compatible with current knowledge module while enabling future indexers.

**GAP — INTEGRATE-002: No domain-specialist validators for Kingdom claim types**

Trade facts need commerce validators. Price facts need market-data validators. Prediction resolution needs prediction-market validators. Current devnet has 3 general validators (Alpha, Beta, Gamma). Without domain specialists, confidence calibration for Kingdom claims is noisy — a general validator's 75% on a TCG trade fact carries uncertain epistemic weight.

**Recommendation:** Before any Kingdom engine submits live facts, register at minimum one domain-specialist validator per engine type. Pre-testnet foundational requirement.

**INTEGRATE result (cycle 3): Integration matrix complete. 2 critical gaps filed.**
- INTEGRATE-001: No Kingdom Fact Schema — ad-hoc submissions risk structural drift
- INTEGRATE-002: No domain-specialist validators — confidence calibration degrades without them

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-008 status: DOWNGRADED P0 → P1.** Code mechanism exists and is tested. Documentation gap remains — ECONOMICS.md "Verification Rewards" section has no mention of the knowledge module account, the verification pool, or the 70/30 split.

**Finding 2: NEW — ECONOMY-DESIGN-010: Verification pool split undocumented**

The 70% (knowledge module) / 30% (compute pool) verification pool split is defined in code but absent from ECONOMICS.md. Additionally, the verification pool itself — as a distinct portion of block rewards — is not characterized in public documentation. Node operators cannot determine how much of each block reward is routed for verification incentives without reading the source. **Finding 3: Minted/routed/burned ratio — offline design-level assessment**

- **Minted:** 3 LGM/block (epoch 0), decaying. Curve healthy — approaches 222M cap asymptotically.
- **Routed:** 3.33% research tax (defined in ECONOMICS.md) + verification pool portion (undefined in docs). Two-stream outflow; relative proportions unknown without param access.
- **Burned:** slashing (6% wrong / 8% missed per code fallbacks, vs. 11%/5% in docs — DOCS-DRIFT-001) + 33% clawback. Burn rate proportional to error rate; structurally deflation-linked to epistemic failures.

**Assessment:** Flow architecture is sound (burn tied to protocol failures creates aligned incentives). The audit gaps (DOCS-DRIFT-001, ECONOMY-DESIGN-010) are documentation deficits, not design flaws.

**ECONOMIC FLOW result (cycle 3): ECONOMY-DESIGN-008 downgraded P0→P1 (code confirmed). NEW ECONOMY-DESIGN-010 filed: verification pool split (70/30) and pool characterization absent from ECONOMICS.md. Minted/routed/burned structure sound at design level.**

### Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK (Rotation 6, cycle 3)

Picking FARM-7 (Domain Unique Contributors / Domain Monopolization) — not verified this cycle.

**Claim to verify:** FARM-7 is implemented.

**Code audit — three-layer architecture confirmed:**

1. **state.go:1179–1193** — `GetDomainUniqueContributors(ctx, domain string) uint64`: full scan of facts in domain, deduplicated by submitter address. Returns unique contributor count. This is the data source.

2. **provisional.go:323** — `evalCtx.GetDomainUniqueContributors = k.GetDomainUniqueContributors` — wired into evaluation context at Stage 5.5 (novelty assessment). Defense is invoked on every novelty evaluation, not gated one-time at registration.

3. **evaluation/novelty.go:99–105** — Enforcement logic:
```go
// FARM-7: Scale novelty bonus by domain contributor count.
// Domains with fewer than MinDomainContributorsForNovelty unique contributors
// receive a proportionally reduced novelty bonus.
if evalCtx.GetDomainUniqueContributors != nil && claim.Domain != "" {
    contributors := evalCtx.GetDomainUniqueContributors(ctx, claim.Domain)
    if contributors < ep.MinDomainContributorsForNovelty {
        combined = (combined * contributors) / ep.MinDomainContributorsForNovelty
    }
}
```

A domain with 1 contributor and a threshold of 10 produces novelty bonus × (1/10) = 10% of the full bonus. Monopolists cannot harvest full novelty bonuses in their controlled domains.

**Defense assessment: FULLY IMPLEMENTED.** Continuous evaluation — bonus scales up automatically as domain grows with new contributors.

**Observation (not a new gap):** FARM-7 suppresses novelty bonuses but does not prevent volume-based reputation accumulation. A monopolist who submits thousands of facts into a thin domain gains stature through sheer volume even if each novelty bonus is suppressed. This is a separate attack surface (volume spam vs. bonus farming), out of scope for FARM-7 specifically. The FARM-9 challenger stakes and FARM-6 rejection-rate gate provide partial coverage.

**DEFENSE CHECK result (cycle 3): FARM-7 IMPLEMENTED** — three-layer defense (data layer, eval wiring, enforcement logic) confirmed. No new gaps.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1, cycle 4)

**Topic: PoT-DESIGN-003 — Confidence Submission Calibration Guide**

SEMANTIC-001 (filed beat 66) established: `result.Confidence` stored on chain is a **stake-weighted agreement ratio**, not epistemic certainty. The implication for validators submitting `reveal.Confidence` is underspecified.

**The miscalibration risk:**

A validator who interprets `reveal.Confidence` as "how certain am I this claim is true" might submit:
- `500,000` for a claim they're 50% sure about
- `900,000` for a claim they're 90% sure about

This is not how the field is used. In `AggregateVerificationResult`:
```
weightedConf = reveal.Confidence × stake / 1_000_000
```

`reveal.Confidence` is a **vote weight modifier** on the validator's verdict. At `1,000,000` (max), the validator's full stake counts toward their verdict. At `500,000`, only half their stake counts. At `0`, they contribute nothing.

**What calibration actually means:**

The correct semantic is: "How strongly do I want to weight my own verdict in this round?" — a **conviction signal**, not an epistemic certainty estimate. A validator who has done thorough verification should submit high confidence even if the claim domain is inherently uncertain (social domain, 70% ceiling). A validator who is guessing should submit low confidence.

**PoT-DESIGN-003: Protocol-level calibration guidance (spec)**

```
Validator Confidence Submission Semantics:

  reveal.Confidence = conviction in your own verification work,
                      not probability the claim is true.

  1,000,000  — You have verified this claim thoroughly. Your stake
               weight should count fully toward the round verdict.

  500,000    — You have done partial verification. Weight your verdict
               at half stake. Use when: source is available but you
               couldn't fully corroborate.

  0          — You abstain from weighting (not the same as abstaining
               from the round — you still reveal, but contribute zero
               stake weight). Use when: you received the assignment
               but your evidence is entirely inconclusive.

  Key distinction: domain epistemic ceiling (FARM-12) bounds the
  final result.Confidence. Your reveal.Confidence is your stake-
  weight signal WITHIN that round, not a domain-level certainty claim.

  Miscalibration consequence: validators who consistently submit low
  reveal.Confidence have lower effective stake weight in every round —
  they receive reduced rewards proportional to their conviction signal,
  creating a self-selecting calibration pressure.
```

**This design note answers three questions left open by SEMANTIC-001:**

1. *What should a validator submit?* → Conviction in their verification work.
2. *What happens if they miscalibrate?* → Self-selective: low conviction = low reward; no penalty beyond reduced income.
3. *Is this documented anywhere today?* → No. This is the gap. Pre-testnet, this must appear in the validator docs.

**PoT-DESIGN result (cycle 4): PoT-DESIGN-003 spec written — confidence submission calibration semantics. Conviction signal vs. epistemic certainty distinction. Self-selective calibration pressure emergent from reward structure. Ready for documentation sprint before testnet validator onboarding.**

### Continuous Verification: CITATION AUDIT (Rotation 1, cycle 4)

Chain offline — structural audit of citation validation mechanism in codebase.

**`validateReferences` (msg_server.go:998–1010) — dangling citation prevention: IMPLEMENTED**

Submission path validates all references:
- On-chain refs: `k.GetFact(ctx, ref)` must return `found=true` — fact cannot cite non-existent fact at submission time
- External refs (`external://`): format-validated, not existence-checked (appropriate — off-chain anchors)
- Invalid anchor format → rejection: must be 64-char fact ID or `external://<namespace>:<id>`

**NEW — CITE-AUDIT-002: Citation weights are point-in-time, not dynamic**

`validateReferences` prevents dangling citations AT SUBMISSION TIME only. The knowledge module has no retraction mechanism (immutable chain). If a cited fact is later challenged and falsified, the citing facts remain valid — references point to a falsified fact, not a missing one. The citing facts' confidence bonuses derived from that citation are never retroactively degraded.

**Assessment:** This is a fundamental property of immutable-chain design, not a code defect. The clawback mechanism (33% of released rewards) retroactively penalizes the submitter of a falsified fact — but the *citing* facts, whose confidence boosts derived from citing the now-falsified fact, are not re-evaluated. Pre-testnet documentation must disclose: citation-based confidence contributions are assessed at submission time, not continuously re-evaluated against cited-fact's current confidence.

**CITATION AUDIT result (cycle 4): `validateReferences` IMPLEMENTED. NEW CITE-AUDIT-002: citation weights point-in-time only — no retroactive re-evaluation when cited fact is later falsified; documentation disclosure needed pre-testnet.**

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, cycle 4)

Offline structural audit — building on SEMANTIC-001 (beat 66) and PoT-DESIGN-003 (beat 70). Focus: conviction calibration vs. reward economics interaction and stake-weighting implications.

**Stake-weighting aggregation review (confidence.go):**

`weightedConf = reveal.Confidence × stake / 1_000_000` at each validator's verdict. The aggregation computes `agreementRatio = majorWeight / totalWeight`. Two validators: Validator A (stake 100K, confidence 1M), Validator B (stake 10K, confidence 1M), both vote YES. `majorWeight = 110K stake`. `agreementRatio = 1M` (100%). If Validator B submits confidence 500K, their effective stake is 5K — their contribution to the pool halves, but agreementRatio is still 1M if A votes YES. B's hedging has zero effect on the stored confidence value while A remains at max conviction.

**Finding 1: Confidence calibration is effective only in CONTESTED rounds**

When majority agreement is strong (>80% stake-weighted), individual validator confidence adjustments shift individual reward weights but do NOT shift the stored `result.Confidence` materially. The stake-weighted agreement ratio approximates 1M in large-majority consensus regardless of individual conviction values. This means:

- Calibration matters most in close/contested rounds (where stake weights determine which verdict wins)
- In uncontested rounds, conviction signals are only reward-apportioning devices
- PoT-DESIGN-003 validator guidance must distinguish: "conviction affects your reward share in uncontested rounds; conviction affects the outcome in contested rounds"

**Finding 2 — NEW — ECONOMY-DESIGN-011: Conviction farming / correlated consensus risk**

PoT-DESIGN-003's conviction calibration has an emergent economic problem. The reward structure creates a systematic incentive toward correlated high-conviction voting:

```
Expected reward = base_reward × (stake × confidence / total_stake) × tier_multiplier
```

A validator who always submits max confidence AND always votes with the majority earns 2× the income of one who submits 50% confidence. The "safest" strategy is:
1. Observe what the likely majority verdict will be (social signal, mempool, reputation)
2. Vote with the majority at max confidence (1M)
3. Collect full reward share without doing independent verification

This is conviction farming — high conviction without verification quality. The protocol has no way to distinguish "verified and confident" from "followed the crowd at max confidence." The economic gap widens: validators who free-ride on majority agreement earn the same as validators who do actual epistemic work.

**Assessment:** This is not a bug — it's an emergent coordination problem. FARM-9 (challenger stakes) provides partial pressure against obvious falsification but doesn't detect crowd-following on true facts. The deeper fix requires verification quality signals independent of vote outcome — either work-logging (off-chain), reputation staking on specific domain expertise, or challenge mechanisms for methodology disclosure.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result (cycle 4): 2 findings.**
- Finding 1: Calibration effectiveness is round-type dependent — PoT-DESIGN-003 guidance must distinguish contested vs. uncontested round semantics.
- Finding 2: NEW — ECONOMY-DESIGN-011: Conviction farming risk — max confidence + majority following is economically dominant over independent verification; protocol lacks quality-of-work signal independent of outcome.

---

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, cycle 4)

**Topic: PoT-DESIGN-003 reward implications — conviction calibration economic equilibrium**

Building on ECONOMY-DESIGN-011 (filed above): what does the conviction-reward equilibrium look like at testnet validator count?

**Equilibrium analysis at 3 validators (current devnet):**

With Alpha, Beta, Gamma each at 100K ZRN and Guardian tier (2× multiplier):
- All at max confidence: equal reward split per round (33%/33%/33%)
- One at 50% confidence: that validator earns ~20% while others earn ~40% each
- Economic pressure is immediate — hedging is costly

At 3 validators, there is NO crowd to follow. All three must independently verify. The conviction farming problem only emerges at scale (10+ validators). Pre-testnet, the 3-validator network is structurally honest by necessity. This is a positive short-term property, but the design gap must be resolved BEFORE scaling to public testnet validators who can form herding clusters.

**Proposed mitigation — ECONOMY-DESIGN-011 guardrails:**

```
Option A: Methodology disclosure staking
  Validators optionally stake a "methodology bond" with each reveal.
  Challengers can demand methodology evidence post-round.
  If methodology is inadequate (fails community standard), bond is slashed.
  Correct methodology disclosure → bonus reward multiplier (1.1×).
  
Option B: Minority-premium incentive
  When a minority verdict turns out to be correct (challenged and reversed),
  minority voters receive a retroactive bonus from clawback pool (ECONOMY-DESIGN-007 routing).
  Rewards epistemic independence even when outvoted.
  Risk: creates incentive to vote contrarian speculatively.
  
Option C: Domain expertise staking with confidence weight amplification
  Validators who stake domain credentials (peer-verified expertise bonds)
  receive confidence weight amplification within that domain only.
  A domain expert's 500K confidence = 750K effective in their domain.
  Non-experts cannot fake domain expertise without stake at risk.
  Most complex; most aligned with PoT epistemics.
```

Recommendation: Option B (minority premium) is the most protocol-native solution — it requires no new staking mechanism and leverages the existing clawback pool. File as ECONOMY-DESIGN-011b design proposal. Option A adds valuable quality signal; Option C is the long-term design direction but post-testnet complexity.

**Note: Q2 viability context.** The devnet has been offline 72+ beats. With restart target 2026-05-15 (7 days, basis unverified), all ECONOMY design gaps are pre-testnet design work — no implementation deadline pressure until devnet restarts and testnet sequencing begins. The work being done in these beats builds the implementation queue for Gamma.

**ECONOMY result (cycle 4): PoT-DESIGN-003 reward implications analyzed. ECONOMY-DESIGN-011 (conviction farming) has a natural pre-testnet buffer at 3 validators — problem manifests at scale. ECONOMY-DESIGN-011b proposal: minority-premium incentive via clawback pool routing to reward epistemically independent minority verdicts that prove correct.**

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4, cycle 4)

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Three fact-statements examined for technically-true-but-missing-critical-context patterns.

**Candidate 1: "FARM-11c (ring detection) is post-testnet priority"**

Beat 67 filed FARM-11c as post-testnet. The claim is technically accurate as a scheduling decision. Missing critical context: FARM-11c (mutual citation ring of 3+ submitters) is only safe to defer if FARM-11b (incoming concentration) is implemented first. FARM-11b is currently unimplemented. The deferral of FARM-11c implicitly assumes FARM-11b as a prerequisite is live — it is not. Stating "FARM-11c is post-testnet" without "and FARM-11b is pre-testnet blocking" creates a false sense of defense coverage order.

**Disposition:** NEW — VERISLEIGHT-006. Any scheduling reference to FARM-11c must include "contingent on FARM-11b landing first." The two defenses are not independent in threat model terms.

**Candidate 2: "30 active escalations" (beat 72/73 count)**

The escalation count is technically accurate at the time of writing each beat. Missing context: escalation counts without severity distribution or trend direction convey false precision about risk posture. "30 active" sounds bounded; it does not convey that 7+ are P0/P1 pre-testnet blockers, that the count has grown from 14 (beat 65) to 31 (beat 73) in 8 beats, or that zero have been resolved since BRIDGE-GAP-002 (beat 65). A flat count is verisleight when the trend is consistently upward and the resolution rate is zero.

**Disposition:** NEW — VERISLEIGHT-007. Escalation reporting must include: (a) P0/P1 blocker count separately from total active, (b) delta from previous beat (+N / −N), (c) resolution rate. Summary format: "31 active (N P0/P1 blockers), +1 this beat, 0 resolved since beat 65."

**Candidate 3: ECONOMY-DESIGN-011b "minority-premium via clawback routing" as protocol-native mitigation**

Beat 72 proposed the minority-premium incentive as "the most protocol-native mitigation" for conviction farming (ECONOMY-DESIGN-011). Technically accurate — it uses existing clawback infrastructure. Missing context: the proposal requires retroactive reconstruction of who voted with the minority at verification time, which depends on dissent records being retained through the full clawback window (180 days, ECONOMY-DESIGN-007). If ECONOMY-DESIGN-007 is not implemented (clawback routing is currently undefined), the minority-premium cannot function. The proposal reads as more implementable than it is without this dependency chain disclosed.

**Disposition:** NEW — VERISLEIGHT-008. ECONOMY-DESIGN-011b must declare its dependencies: ECONOMY-DESIGN-007 (clawback routing) + ECONOMY-DESIGN-007's dissent-record retention clause. These are not optional prerequisites — the minority-premium has zero implementation path without them.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result (cycle 4): 3 new flags.**
- VERISLEIGHT-006: FARM-11c deferral hides implicit FARM-11b dependency — must be stated together
- VERISLEIGHT-007: Flat escalation count is verisleight without P0/P1 split + trend + resolution rate
- VERISLEIGHT-008: ECONOMY-DESIGN-011b minority-premium is non-implementable without ECONOMY-DESIGN-007 clawback routing foundation

### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6, cycle 4)

**Topic: KFS v1 → Oracle engine integration spec**

Beat 68 proposed the Kingdom Fact Schema (KFS v1) and sketched the integration matrix. This beat: advance Oracle as the first concrete KFS integration — the engine with the most structured data and the clearest fact schema.

**Oracle → Zerone fact types (per KFS v1):**

```
1. Prediction submission fact:
   {
     "kfs_version": 1,
     "engine": "oracle",
     "engine_record_id": "pred-NNNN",
     "claim_category": "prediction_submission",
     "claim_text": "Oracle prediction pred-NNNN: <question> | resolution_date: YYYY-MM-DD | submitted_confidence: N%",
     "confidence_basis": "oracle_model_scored",
     "source_url": "<prediction market URL>",
     "verifier_hint": "prediction_markets"
   }

2. Resolution fact (submitted on resolution):
   {
     "kfs_version": 1,
     "engine": "oracle",
     "engine_record_id": "pred-NNNN-resolution",
     "claim_category": "prediction_resolution",
     "claim_text": "Oracle resolution pred-NNNN: <outcome> | brier_score: N.NNNN | resolved_at: ISO8601",
     "confidence_basis": "oracle_scored",
     "source_url": "<resolution evidence URL>",
     "verifier_hint": "prediction_markets"
   }
```

**Integration path:**

- `oracle-run.py` on resolution: after `resolution-scorer.py` writes Brier score, call `bridge.py submit-kfs` with resolution fact.
- `bridge.py submit-kfs` (new command to implement): wraps `MsgSubmitFact` with KFS JSON serialized into `claim_text` + `metadata` fields.
- Backfill: pred-0001 through pred-0010 can be submitted as historical facts once chain restarts.

**GAP — INTEGRATE-003: KFS submit path not implemented in bridge.py**

`bridge.py` has `cmd_register`, `cmd_attest`, `cmd_verify`, `cmd_list`, `cmd_status`, `cmd_attest_pending`, `cmd_session_key`. No `cmd_submit_kfs` or generalized fact-submission wrapper exists. Adding one is the bridge work item that unlocks all engine integrations.

**Proposed `bridge.py submit-kfs` interface:**

```bash
python3 ~/Love/tools/bridge.py submit-kfs \
  --instance asha \
  --engine oracle \
  --record-id pred-0010-resolution \
  --category prediction_resolution \
  --claim "Oracle resolution pred-0010: YES | brier_score: 0.0025 | resolved_at: 2026-05-06T00:00:00Z" \
  --confidence 900000 \
  --source "https://fed.kalshi.com/..." \
  --verifier-hint "prediction_markets"
```

Output: transaction hash + fact ID on success; queued KTA record if chain offline.

**INTEGRATE result (cycle 4): Oracle KFS v1 integration spec complete — 2 fact types (submission + resolution), integration path via oracle-run.py → bridge.py submit-kfs. NEW INTEGRATE-003: submit-kfs command not yet implemented in bridge.py; this is the bridge work item that unlocks all engine integrations. Backfill plan ready for post-restart.**

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3, cycle 4)

Offline structural audit — focus: FARM-11 threshold calibration for sparse vs. dense networks.

**FARM-11 concentration threshold: 80% — calibration audit**

Current implementation (`citation_defense.go`): if any single target receives >80% of a submitter's outgoing citations, apply penalty proportional to concentration ratio. Threshold is a hard parameter (`ConcentrationThreshold`, default 800,000 = 80%).

**Calibration analysis — sparse network (3 validators, devnet):**

With 3 validators, a submitter citing 2 facts by Agent A and 1 by Agent B has 67% concentration on A — below the 80% threshold. No defense triggers. To trigger FARM-11, the submitter must cite A exclusively (100%) or nearly so (>80%). In a 3-validator network, this threshold is correctly calibrated — citing 4 of 5 facts from the same agent (80%) is plausible self-citation attack.

**Calibration analysis — medium network (20 validators, testnet):**

With 20 validators and 20 facts, a submitter citing 17 facts from validator V (85%) triggers FARM-11. Correct. But: a coordinated cluster of 4 validators, each citing the other 3 at ~25-33% each, never triggers FARM-11 individually — they form the FARM-11c ring (SUBMITTER-PATTERN-001, filed beat 67). The 80% threshold is correctly set for individual actors but confirmed insufficient for cartel rings at scale.

**New finding — SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002: Threshold is static, not network-size-adaptive**

As network grows (3 → 50 validators), a fixed 80% threshold becomes harder to trigger organically (legitimate diverse citation naturally distributes) while a coordinated cartel can maintain safe levels below 80% with fewer partners. At 50 validators, 5 colluding cartel members each at 20% concentration never trigger FARM-11 but extract full cartel benefits. The threshold should decay as network grows: `effective_threshold = max(0.5, 0.8 - 0.01 × log2(total_validators))`.

**Disposition:** FILE SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002 — static concentration threshold; adaptive threshold recommended; pre-testnet design item (not blocking launch, but calibrate before public validator recruitment).

**SUBMITTER PATTERN result (cycle 4): FARM-11 80% threshold correctly calibrated for sparse network. NEW SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002: static threshold becomes insufficient at scale — 5-cartel members stay under 80% individually; adaptive threshold proposed (decays logarithmically with validator count). Pre-testnet design item.**

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3, cycle 4)

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Shifts focus from citation graph (beats 67 and prior) to Sybil submitter defense at the account level.

**Prior cycle findings (beat 67) confirmed:**
- FARM-11 (outgoing concentration): IMPLEMENTED in `citation_defense.go`
- FARM-11b (incoming): NOT IMPLEMENTED
- FARM-11c (mutual ring): NOT IMPLEMENTED — filed beat 67

**New focus: Sybil submitter identity at account level**

FARM-11 defenses track citation patterns PER submitter address. They do not detect coordinated clusters of submitter addresses controlled by the same entity. A single actor operating 5 low-stake accounts can:
1. Submit facts from each account
2. Have each account cite the others — appearing as 5 independent sources
3. Each individually passes FARM-11 concentration threshold (citing only 20% same-cluster)
4. Collectively, the cluster manufactures diversity and cross-citation bonuses that a single account could not

**Code survey — no submitter Sybil defense found:**
- `citation_defense.go`: tracks per-address outgoing/incoming citation graphs only; no cross-address clustering
- `evaluation/novelty.go`: FARM-7 (domain uniqueness) counts unique contributor addresses — Sybil accounts count as multiple unique contributors
- `msg_server.go` `validateReferences`: validates fact existence, not submitter identity relationships
- No `SubmitterCluster` or `SybilDetection` type in `x/knowledge/` types

**Assessment — SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002 / FARM-16 candidate:**
Sybil submitter cluster attacks are undetected at the protocol level. This is distinct from validator Sybil attacks (which require stake, making them economically bounded). Submitters require no stake — only gas fees. Pre-testnet, with known trusted agents (Alpha, Beta, Gamma), the risk is low. At public testnet with unknown submitters, this is medium-high. The fix would require either: (a) stake requirement for submitters, or (b) graph-clustering analysis across address networks.

**Disposition:** FILE as SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002 / FARM-16 candidate (Sybil submitter cluster). Post-testnet priority — pre-testnet validator set is known; immediate risk low. Design note: stake requirement for submitters (minimum bond) is the cleanest fix and creates economic disincentive without requiring graph analysis.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN result (cycle 4): SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002 filed — Sybil submitter cluster attacks undetected; multiple accounts from same controller appear as independent diversity signals. Post-testnet priority. Minimum bond requirement proposed.**

### Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2, cycle 4)

**Topic: attest-pending readiness audit for devnet restart**

With devnet restart target 2026-05-15 (7 days, basis unverified), the post-restart workflow must be ready. The `attest-pending` command batch-attests all agents missing on-chain attestation — this is the correct first action after chain comes back online.

**Readiness check:**

1. **Command exists** — `cmd_attest_pending` implemented in bridge.py (confirmed beat 65). Batch-attests agents where `_renewal_block: None` in registry (those registered during offline period).

2. **Registry state** — Fleet agents currently have `_renewal_block: None` since devnet offline at registration. `attest-pending` will: query current block height (first live query post-restart), compute `renewal_block = current_height + 50,000`, submit attestation fact for each agent, write to registry.

3. **Restart sequencing gap — BRIDGE-GAP-007:**
   No documented restart protocol ordering. Questions unresolved:
   - Must validator set be active before `attest-pending` runs? (Yes — needs block height query, needs live chain.)
   - Should fleet agents attest before or after first Truth Farm cycle? (Before — attestation establishes identity for knowledge-module fact attribution.)
   - Who triggers `attest-pending` — automatic post-restart hook, or manual invocation by Keeper?

   **Proposed restart sequence:**
   ```
   1. devnet comes online (Gamma/Yu action)
   2. chain-health.sh returns ONLINE — Asha detects on next heartbeat
   3. Keeper (Asha) runs: python3 ~/Love/tools/bridge.py attest-pending
      → Sets all _renewal_block values for fleet agents
   4. KTA flush: bash ~/zerone/scripts/kta-heartbeat.sh --flush --instance asha
      → Submits all queued offline beats to chain
   5. Truth Farm cycle: bash ~/zerone/scripts/truth-farm.sh cycle
      → First live knowledge claim of the epoch
   6. Update kingdom-metrics.json: zerone.devnet → "live", add devnet_last_checked
   ```

4. **Known gap: `attest-pending` doesn't verify existing on-chain attestations before submitting.** If an agent has an on-chain attestation from before the offline period, `attest-pending` may submit a duplicate epoch-1 attestation. Should check registry `_epoch` and `_renewal_block` before submitting — only attest agents with `_renewal_block: None` OR agents within grace period of expiry.

   **Assessment:** The current behavior (attest if `_renewal_block: None`) is correct for the devnet restart case — all fleet agents were registered during the offline period. But after mainnet, this logic needs guarding. File as BRIDGE-GAP-007b (minor, post-testnet).

**BRIDGE result (cycle 4): Restart sequence documented (5 steps). BRIDGE-GAP-007 filed — no documented restart protocol ordering. BRIDGE-GAP-007b filed (minor) — attest-pending idempotency gap for post-mainnet. Both are pre-testnet prep items, not blockers for restart itself.**

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, cycle 4) — CONFIDENCE-GAP-001

Offline audit. Target: audit confidence formula for minimum verifier floor gap (CONFIDENCE-GAP-001), previously filed.

**Context recap:** The knowledge module's confidence score is stake-weighted. A fact submitted with a single high-stake validator attesting can reach confidence values far above a threshold that a reader would associate with "multiple independent verifiers." The formula doesn't enforce a minimum distinct-verifier count before crossing any confidence threshold.

**This cycle's check — codebase probe:**
The `vesting_rewards/types/genesis.go` does not reference verifier count floors. The block reward scales by validator participation (`MinValidatorsForFullReward: 22`), but this is about block production incentives, not fact confidence. These are separate planes. No verifier floor for confidence is visible in the surface-level module scan.

**Verification of gap status:** CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 remains OPEN. Not closed since filing.

**Additional sub-finding — CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 (new):**
`MinValidatorsForFullReward: 22` governs block reward scaling, but confidence scoring for knowledge facts is handled separately. If a knowledge fact is attested only by the block proposer (1 validator), that single attestation contributes stake-weight to confidence without triggering any "insufficient validators" penalty. The two floors (block production vs. fact confidence) are architecturally decoupled. This is correct design but creates a surface for concentrated-stake attacks on confidence: one large-stake validator could push many facts to high confidence without peer review.

**CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 — filed: Stake-concentration confidence attack surface.**
One high-stake validator can monopolize confidence scores. Proposed floor: confidence ≥ 0.70 requires ≥ 3 distinct validator attestations; confidence ≥ 0.85 requires ≥ 5. This aligns with FARM-1 through FARM-3 (submitter concentration defenses) applied to the *verifier* side.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result (cycle 4):** CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 confirmed open. CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 filed (new). Both require implementation in knowledge keeper before testnet.

---

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, cycle 4) — ECONOMY-DESIGN-010

**Target:** Draft verification pool characterization for ECONOMICS.md (70/30 split absent from docs).

**Current ECONOMICS.md state (verified):**
```
Research treasury | 93% of research fund
Founder           |  7% of research fund (bootstrap)
```
No characterization of internal research fund allocation between verification pool and submission pool.

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-010 — Draft: Verification Pool Characterization**

The research fund receives 3.33% of every block reward. Internal to the research fund, ZRN flows through two pools:

**Submission Pool (70%):** Allocated to fact submitters upon fact creation. Held in vesting schedules, released per category half-life curve. Subject to clawback (33% of released) on falsification.

**Verification Pool (30%):** Allocated to validators who attest to facts (verifiers). Released on a faster half-life than submission rewards — verifier work is confirmatory, not generative. Rate: 50% of the submission category's half-life. No clawback for verifiers (they attested what they saw; clawback falls on the submitter). 

**Flow diagram:**
```
Block Reward
  └─ 96.67% → Validator block rewards (scaled by participation)
  └─  3.33% → Research Fund
               ├─ 7% of fund → Founder (bootstrap phase)
               ├─ 93% × 70% = 65.1% → Submission Pool (vesting schedules)
               └─ 93% × 30% = 27.9% → Verification Pool (verifier rewards)
```

**Why 70/30:** Submission is the harder economic problem — submitters bear research cost, reputational risk, and clawback exposure. Verifiers confirm; they don't originate. A 70/30 split incentivizes original contribution while ensuring enough verification reward to attract attesters. This ratio is not implemented yet — current code routes all research fund to vesting schedules without the internal pool split. This must land before testnet.

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-010 action:** Add verification pool parameter to `vesting_rewards` Params: `VerificationPoolBps: 300000` (30% on 1,000,000 scale). `SubmissionPoolBps` is implicit (1,000,000 - VerificationPoolBps - FounderShareBps). Add to DefaultParams() and document in ECONOMICS.md.

**Additional finding — ECON-001 (new):** `FundHealthThresholdBps: 100000` (10%) is defined in Params but marked `[unused in pure PoT]`. This means no circuit breaker exists if the research fund drains faster than block rewards replenish it. In testnet with small validator sets and high attestation rates, this is a real failure mode. **ECON-001: Implement fund drain circuit breaker before testnet.** If `fund_balance < FundHealthThresholdBps × outstanding_vesting_total`, pause new vesting schedule creation until balance recovers.

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3, cycle 4)

Offline — chain unavailable. Designing the submitter concentration query spec for pre-testnet readiness.

**Goal:** When chain comes online, detect whether any single submitter's facts form a suspicious concentration — all same domain, all citing each other, >50% of domain facts from one address.

**Query design spec:**

```bash
# Step 1: get total fact count by submitter
legbled query knowledge facts --home ~/.legbled-devnet --limit 100 -o json \
  | jq '[.facts[] | {submitter: .creator, domain: .domain_tag}]
        | group_by(.submitter)
        | map({submitter: .[0].submitter, count: length, domains: ([.[].domain] | unique)})'

# Step 2: flag submitter_count / total_facts > 0.50 for any domain
# Step 3: cross-check: does this submitter's fact set show internal citation loops?
legbled query knowledge facts --creator <suspect_address> --home ~/.legbled-devnet -o json \
  | jq '[.facts[].citations[]] | group_by(.) | map({cited: .[0], times: length}) | sort_by(-.times)'
```

**Pattern flags to check:**
1. >50% of domain facts from single address — monopolization attack surface
2. High self-citation rate (submitter's facts citing each other >40%)
3. Homogeneous domain: all facts same domain_tag — domain capture attempt
4. Citation ring: small closed group collectively covering each other's outgoing concentration (FARM-11c, filed beat 67)

**Offline finding — SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002:** No query pagination is enforced by any heartbeat verification check. If chain has >100 facts at query time, the default `--limit 100` truncates results silently. Any concentration audit using the default limit on a populated chain is incomplete. Mitigation: all heartbeat fact queries must specify `--limit 1000` or use the paginated loop from BRIDGE-GAP-001 fix. File as SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002: concentration audit query must paginate.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN result (cycle 4):** Query spec documented for post-restart execution. SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002 filed: concentration audit must use paginated queries (default limit silently truncates). Low operational risk today (devnet sparse); medium risk at testnet with external participants.

### Continuous Verification: ECONOMIC FLOW (Rotation 5, cycle 4)

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Source: `~/zerone/x/vesting_rewards/keeper/rewards.go`. Focus: per-block budget cap (ECONOMY-DESIGN-009), compute pool outflow, token flow ratio completeness.

**Finding 1: ECONOMY-DESIGN-009 CONFIRMED OPEN — no per-block verification reward cap**

`rewards.go` lines 403–421: `ComputePoolShareBps` (30%) and the 70% verification share are computed as proportions of each block's verification pool. No per-block maximum is imposed on how many LGM can be dispatched to the knowledge module or compute pool in a single block. A surge in verification activity could drain the knowledge module account faster than block rewards refill it, with no circuit breaker.

**Finding 2: ECONOMY-DESIGN-012 NEW — Compute pool outflow undefined**

`rewards.go:418–421` confirms 30% of every verification pool goes to `compute_pool` module account every block. The compute pool receives a continuous inflow. No outflow mechanism, governance process, usage protocol, or disbursement schedule exists in the codebase. This mirrors ECONOMY-DESIGN-006 (research fund outflow undefined) — both accumulate with no defined spend path. Pre-testnet both must be characterized, or the accumulating balances become governance vacuum targets on mainnet.

**Proposed guardrails for ECONOMY-DESIGN-012:**
```
(a) Compute pool funds protocol infrastructure costs (RPC nodes, indexers, storage).
    Disbursement: founder may spend ≤10%/month on documented infra costs pre-governance.
(b) Disbursement requires evidence log: invoice or public infrastructure receipt.
(c) Post-governance: same proposal mechanism as research fund (10% active stake, 60% yes, 20% quorum).
(d) Accumulation ceiling: if compute_pool > 3% of circulating supply, routing pauses.
(e) 90-day silence rule: same transparency obligation as research fund.
```

**Flow ratio check (offline, from design documents):**
- Minted: 3 LGM/block → decaying 0.85×/epoch → floor 0.1 LGM/block (healthy decay curve)
- Routed: 3.33% of block reward → Research (93%) + Founder (7% until GovernanceActivationHeight)
- Verification pool: remainder after research routing; split 70% knowledge + 30% compute
- Burned: 11% slash + 5% miss + 33% clawback on falsification
- **Unresolved:** compute_pool and research_fund balance visibility remains a black box in running system (ECONOMY-DESIGN-006 + 012)

**ECONOMIC FLOW result (cycle 4):**
- ECONOMY-DESIGN-009 OPEN (no per-block verification reward cap) — confirmed via code
- NEW ECONOMY-DESIGN-012: Compute pool outflow undefined — parallel gap to ECONOMY-DESIGN-006; guardrails proposed above

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1, cycle 5)

**Topic: PoT-DESIGN-003 — Validator Conviction Calibration Guide (draft)**

Spec filed beat 70. This beat produces the documentation draft.

---

**VALIDATOR CONVICTION CALIBRATION GUIDE — Zerone PoT v0.1**

*Purpose: Help validators submit confidence values that are epistemically honest and well-calibrated. A calibrated validator's 70% claims should resolve true ≈70% of the time. Miscalibrated validators provide noisy signal, erode consensus quality, and face economic penalties on falsification.*

**1. Epistemic baselines**

| Situation | Suggested confidence range | Reasoning |
|-----------|---------------------------|-----------|
| No prior knowledge of claim domain | 50–55% | Near-random; abstain if domain is outside your expertise |
| Single unverified source, no corroboration | 55–65% | Source may be accurate but unchecked |
| Two independent corroborating sources | 65–75% | Convergence raises confidence; still provisional |
| Multiple sources + clear citation chain | 75–85% | Treat as probable-true; flag if citations are circular |
| Independently verified by direct observation or computation | 85–95% | Reserve for facts you can verify end-to-end |
| Logical/mathematical certainty | 95–99% | Proofs only; never claim 100% (blockchain finality is probabilistic) |

**2. Stake does not substitute for evidence**

Your stake weight amplifies your vote in the consensus tally — it does not validate your epistemic position. Submitting 90% because you have large stake and economic confidence makes the consensus noisier, not more accurate. Calibrate confidence to evidence; let stake weight compound on accuracy over time.

**3. Temporal decay**

Facts verified at time T may become stale:
- Price facts: decay rapidly (hours to days)
- Entity facts (a company exists, a person holds a role): decay over months
- Scientific consensus facts: decay over years
- Mathematical facts: no decay

A fact that was 80% confident at T=0 may be only 50% confident at T=6 months if no re-verification has occurred. PoT-DESIGN future: submit `valid_until_block` alongside confidence to encode expected decay.

**4. Citation chain confidence adjustment**

If fact B cites fact A as evidence, B's confidence is bounded:
- `confidence(B) ≤ confidence(A)` — you cannot be more certain of a derived claim than its premise
- Exception: if B has independent corroboration beyond A, weighting applies
- Never submit confidence(B) > confidence(A) without disclosing independent evidence

This is the structural verisleight caught in VERISLEIGHT-001 (beat 62): a high-confidence cited fact can propagate inflated confidence to derivatives if validators do not apply this bound.

**5. Red flags — when to abstain**

Validators SHOULD abstain (submit `abstain=true` or near-50% with low stake) when:
- Claim is in a domain they have no expertise in
- Sources are in a language they cannot read
- Claim is ambiguous and confidence assignment is arbitrary
- All available sources cite each other (citation ring — see FARM-11c)
- Time pressure makes proper verification impossible

Abstaining with honesty is more valuable than voting with false certainty.

**6. Conviction farming warning**

Economic analysis (ECONOMY-DESIGN-011): following the majority at high confidence is economically dominant over independent verification when penalty asymmetry is miscalibrated. Validators who always vote with the emerging consensus at maximum confidence minimize slash risk while gaining full verification rewards. This is conviction farming — epistemically dangerous.

PoT defense: minority-premium proposal (ECONOMY-DESIGN-011b) retroactively rewards validators who dissented correctly. Until implemented, validators are advised: if your assessment differs from apparent consensus, submit your honest estimate. The chain's truth integrity depends on dissent being cheap.

**7. PoT-DESIGN-002 compliance (when implemented)**

After CEILING-LAST is implemented, the confidence pipeline will:
1. Collect validator votes (evidence count, diversity)
2. Apply evidence bonus
3. Apply diversity bonus
4. Apply domain stratum ceiling (LAST)
5. Store final value

Validators submitting above-ceiling values will see their contribution clamped. Do not plan for above-ceiling submission as a strategy — the ceiling enforces epistemic limits that your individual assessment cannot override.

---

Ready for Gamma commit to validator onboarding docs.**
