### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, cycle 5)

**Topic: ECONOMY-DESIGN-013 — Meter Health Check Implementation Spec**

Beat 76 identified and filed: no automated meter health threshold checks for the minted/routed/burned ratio. Devnet restart target 2026-05-15 (7 days). The check must exist before restart. This beat: write the implementation spec for `chain-health.sh --economic`.

**Implementation spec — `chain-health.sh --economic` extension:**

```bash
# Add to ~/zerone/scripts/chain-health.sh

check_economic_health() {
  local HOME="${LEGBLED_HOME:-~/.legbled-devnet}"
  local BINARY="${ZERONE_BINARY:-~/zerone/build/legbled}"

  # Query meter values
  local MINTED; MINTED=$($BINARY query vesting_rewards meter minted --home "$HOME" -o json 2>/dev/null | jq -r '.amount // 0')
  local ROUTED; ROUTED=$($BINARY query vesting_rewards meter routed --home "$HOME" -o json 2>/dev/null | jq -r '.amount // 0')
  local BURNED; BURNED=$($BINARY query vesting_rewards meter burned --home "$HOME" -o json 2>/dev/null | jq -r '.amount // 0')

  if [[ "$MINTED" == "0" ]]; then
    echo "ECONOMIC: no data (chain new or query failed)"
    return 1
  fi

  # Health thresholds (proposed ECONOMY-DESIGN-013)
  local ROUTED_PCT=$(( ROUTED * 100 / MINTED ))
  local BURNED_PCT=$(( BURNED * 100 / MINTED ))
  local NET=$(( MINTED - BURNED ))

  echo "ECONOMIC METER:"
  echo "  Minted: $MINTED ulgm"
  echo "  Routed: $ROUTED ulgm (${ROUTED_PCT}% of minted)"
  echo "  Burned: $BURNED ulgm (${BURNED_PCT}% of minted)"
  echo "  Net supply delta: $NET ulgm"

  local STATUS="OK"
  # Routed/Minted: healthy 45-55%; < 45% or > 55% = routing anomaly
  if (( ROUTED_PCT < 45 || ROUTED_PCT > 55 )); then
    echo "  WARNING: Routed ratio ${ROUTED_PCT}% outside [45,55]% band — routing anomaly"
    STATUS="DEGRADED"
  fi
  # Burned/Minted: < 5% healthy; 5-10% warn; > 10% alert; > 20% emergency
  if (( BURNED_PCT > 20 )); then
    echo "  EMERGENCY: Burned ratio ${BURNED_PCT}% > 20% — excessive slashing"
    STATUS="CRITICAL"
  elif (( BURNED_PCT > 10 )); then
    echo "  ALERT: Burned ratio ${BURNED_PCT}% > 10% — elevated slashing"
    STATUS="DEGRADED"
  elif (( BURNED_PCT > 5 )); then
    echo "  WARNING: Burned ratio ${BURNED_PCT}% > 5%"
    [[ "$STATUS" == "OK" ]] && STATUS="WARN"
  fi
  # Net supply must be positive
  if (( NET <= 0 )); then
    echo "  EMERGENCY: net supply non-positive ($NET) — deflation without governance"
    STATUS="CRITICAL"
  fi

  echo "  ECONOMIC STATUS: $STATUS"
  [[ "$STATUS" == "OK" || "$STATUS" == "WARN" ]]
}
```

**Invocation:** `bash ~/zerone/scripts/chain-health.sh --economic 2>/dev/null`

**Pre-testnet checklist item:** Run economic health check within first 100 blocks of devnet restart (2026-05-15). Expected initial state: MINTED > 0, ROUTED ≈50%, BURNED ≈0% (no slashing yet). Any deviation from expected state requires investigation before announcing testnet readiness.

**Additional: integrate into devnet-restart.md**

The devnet restart sequence (BRIDGE-GAP-007, beat 71) should include `chain-health.sh --economic` as step 4 (after chain sync confirmed, before agent attestations): verify economic parameters are sane before agents begin submitting facts.

**ECONOMY result (cycle 5):** ECONOMY-DESIGN-013 implementation spec complete — `check_economic_health()` function ready for Gamma to integrate into chain-health.sh. Pre-testnet checklist item added. Cross-referenced with BRIDGE-GAP-007 devnet restart sequence.

**NEW FINDING — CONFIDENCE-GAP-002: Empty SelectedValidators defaults quorum to 100%**

```go
participationRatio := uint64(1_000_000) // Default 100% if no selection tracking
if len(round.SelectedValidators) > 0 {
    participationRatio = (uint64(len(round.Reveals)) * 1_000_000) / uint64(len(round.SelectedValidators))
}
if participationRatio < params.QuorumThreshold {
    result.Verdict = "insufficient"
```

If `SelectedValidators` is empty (unset), participation defaults to 100%, passing quorum regardless of `params.QuorumThreshold`. A round with 1 reveal and no SelectedValidators set would pass quorum. Attack vector: if a validator selection event fails to populate `SelectedValidators` (race condition, parameter misconfiguration, or validator set churn at round creation), the quorum gate is bypassed silently.

Proposed fix: if `len(SelectedValidators) == 0`, return `insufficient` — no selection recorded means no quorum basis.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result (cycle 5): 1 new gap, 1 confirmed.**
- PoT-DESIGN-002 (evidence + diversity bonuses post-ceiling): confirmed live, Gamma implementation pending
- CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 (NEW): empty SelectedValidators silently passes quorum — should return insufficient when no selection basis exists

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, cycle 6)

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Focus: CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 (empty SelectedValidators silently passes quorum) — the highest pre-testnet risk per beat 79 COORDINATE duty.

**Invariant under test:** For any fact with confidence > 0, there must be at least one validator attestation with positive stake weight in SelectedValidators at verification time.

**Offline formula analysis (from ComputeFactConfidence design):**

The quorum check logic:
```go
quorumMet := len(verifications) >= required
if !quorumMet { return 0, ErrQuorumNotMet }
```

If `required = 0` (computed from active validator count × quorum fraction when active set is empty), then `len(verifications) >= 0` is always true for any `len(verifications)`, including zero. Result: `quorumMet = true`. The function proceeds to accumulate evidence scores, produces a positive confidence value, and stores a fact as "verified" with zero actual attestors.

**CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 confirmation (cycle 6):** The logical path is closed. Empty SelectedValidators → required=0 → quorum passes trivially → confidence computed → fact stored as verified with no human/validator attestation. This is a categorical truth-integrity failure: the chain claims something is verified that no one verified.

**Pre-testnet fix specification (ready for Gamma):**

```go
// In ComputeFactConfidence(), before any quorum computation:
if len(params.SelectedValidators) == 0 {
    return 0, fmt.Errorf("no validators assigned: fact cannot be verified")
}

// In the assignment keeper (before SelectedValidators is committed):
if len(assigned) == 0 {
    return types.ErrInsufficientValidators
}
```

**Defense note:** Both guards are needed. The confidence function guard catches it at computation; the assignment keeper guard catches it upstream before the verification round begins. Belt-and-suspenders because a verified-with-no-validators stored fact cannot be corrected retroactively.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result (cycle 6):** CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 confirmed via logical path closure. Fix specification complete — two-guard approach in confidence function + assignment keeper. Both must land before devnet restart (2026-05-15).

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1, cycle 6)

**Topic: PoT-DESIGN-003 — Fact lifecycle model (provisional → settled → disputed → superseded)**

The current PoT design treats facts as: stored | falsified. No intermediate states. With testnet approaching, this gap becomes operational: validators need to know what happens when evidence contradicts a settled fact.

**Proposed fact lifecycle:**

```
PROVISIONAL  →  SETTLED    →  SUPERSEDED
     ↓               ↓              ↓
REJECTED      DISPUTED    (historical, immutable)
                  ↓
              FALSIFIED (clawback triggered)
```

**State definitions:**

- **PROVISIONAL**: Submitted, awaiting quorum. Confidence computed but chain-height < finality_window. Subject to reversion if quorum fails after tallying.
- **SETTLED**: Quorum met + finality_window elapsed. Confidence value locked. Cannot be mutated; only superseded.
- **DISPUTED**: A new submission directly contradicts a SETTLED fact (same claim address, different evidence hash). Enters dispute resolution window (proposal: 100 blocks). Both facts remain on-chain. PoT adjudicator (highest-stake validator cluster) rules.
- **REJECTED**: A PROVISIONAL fact that failed quorum or was superseded before settling.
- **FALSIFIED**: A SETTLED fact proven fraudulent post-hoc. Triggers 33% clawback from original submitter and verifiers. Fact remains immutable on-chain (history) but flagged `is_falsified=true`.
- **SUPERSEDED**: A SETTLED fact replaced by a newer version with higher evidence weight. Original remains (audit trail); new fact references `supersedes_fact_id`. Reader convention: use highest non-falsified fact for a given claim address.

**Design constraint:** All states are terminal or mono-directional. SETTLED facts cannot return to PROVISIONAL. FALSIFIED facts cannot be rehabilitated (chain integrity). SUPERSEDED facts are historical record, not authoritative.

**Integration with CONFIDENCE-GAP-002:** Any fact that reached SETTLED state under the no-validator bug (pre-fix) should be auto-flagged as SETTLED_UNVERIFIED (a new tag, not a state) at devnet restart. Not retroactively falsified — that would trigger unjust clawbacks — but clearly marked as requiring re-verification. Proposal: `confidence_pipeline_version` field (already recommended in beat 64) carries this distinction.

**PoT-DESIGN-003 escalation:** Gamma — review and extend. The DISPUTED state requires a dispute resolution governance mechanism not yet designed. That is PoT-DESIGN-004 (scoped separately; don't block PoT-DESIGN-003 on it).

**PoT-DESIGN result (cycle 6):** PoT-DESIGN-003 lifecycle model drafted. 6 states defined (PROVISIONAL, SETTLED, SUPERSEDED, REJECTED, DISPUTED, FALSIFIED). SETTLED_UNVERIFIED tag proposed for devnet restart migration. PoT-DESIGN-004 (dispute resolution governance) scoped as follow-on.

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3, cycle 6)

Offline analysis — chain unavailable. Focus: refining the submitter concentration detection query from beat 73 in light of CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 (empty SelectedValidators) and SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002 (pagination truncation).

**Concentration detection — updated query spec (v2):**

```bash
# Step 1: paginated fact retrieval (SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002 fix — avoid default --limit 100)
PAGE=1
while true; do
  BATCH=$($BINARY query knowledge facts --home "$HOME" --limit 100 --offset $((($PAGE-1)*100)) -o json 2>/dev/null)
  COUNT=$(echo "$BATCH" | jq '.facts | length')
  echo "$BATCH" | jq '.facts[] | {creator, domain_tag}' >> /tmp/all_facts.jsonl
  [[ "$COUNT" -lt 100 ]] && break
  PAGE=$(( PAGE + 1 ))
done

# Step 2: concentration analysis
jq -s 'group_by(.creator) | map({submitter: .[0].creator, count: length, domains: ([.[].domain_tag] | unique), domain_count: ([.[].domain_tag] | unique | length)}) | sort_by(-.count)' /tmp/all_facts.jsonl

# Step 3: per-submitter citation loop detection
# Flag submitter where self_citations / total_citations > 0.40
```

**NEW finding — SUBMITTER-PATTERN-003: validator concentration risk at testnet genesis**

At devnet genesis, only Alpha, Beta, and Gamma are registered validators. All facts submitted in the pre-testnet phase originate from these three addresses. A concentration analysis at testnet launch will show 33% per submitter — below the 50% monopoly threshold but constituting 100% of the fact corpus from known parties. External testnet participants will inherit a corpus built entirely by insider submitters. This is not an attack, but creates:
1. Circular citation paths where the only cited facts are from the same 3 addresses
2. Confidence bootstrapping without independent verifier diversity (CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 is amplified)
3. Misleading diversity metrics (FARM-7 counts unique contributors — 3 gives false sense of diversity)

**Disposition — SUBMITTER-PATTERN-003 filed:** Genesis corpus insider concentration. No operational fix pre-testnet; structural mitigation for testnet: announce to external validators that genesis facts are from core team; publish provenance labels on genesis corpus; apply SETTLED_UNVERIFIED tag (PoT-DESIGN-003 beat 80) to flag all pre-external-validator facts as requiring independent re-verification. Post-testnet: set minimum external-validator participation threshold for SETTLED status.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN result (cycle 6):** Query spec v2 updated with pagination fix (SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002) and step 3 self-citation ratio. NEW: SUBMITTER-PATTERN-003 — genesis corpus insider concentration risk at testnet launch; mitigated by SETTLED_UNVERIFIED tagging + provenance labeling; no structural fix pre-testnet.

### Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2, cycle 6)

**Focus: BRIDGE-GAP-002 registry field pre-population readiness**

Beat 71 established that `attest-pending` should batch-attest all agents with `_renewal_block: None`. The candidate task this beat: pre-populate `_renewal_block` and `_epoch` fields in bridge.py registry structure so they exist as typed fields even before chain restart, preventing KeyError on first access post-restart.

**Registry field audit (from bridge.py working tree — file marked M in git status):**

The bridge.py registry uses a dict per agent. The `attest-pending` code path reads `agent['_renewal_block']` — if the key is absent (older registry entries), this raises KeyError. The defensive pattern is: `agent.get('_renewal_block', None)` already in the current implementation (confirmed from prior BRIDGE-GAP-002 work, beat 65). So KeyError is guarded.

**Remaining gap — BRIDGE-GAP-008 (new):** The registry has no schema version field. If bridge.py's expected agent schema changes between devnet offline period and restart, there is no migration path. A registry written at bridge.py v1.2 (current) may be read by bridge.py v1.4 (post-Sprint A) without version negotiation.

**Proposed fix (pre-restart, no chain needed):**

```python
REGISTRY_SCHEMA_VERSION = "1.0"

def _migrate_registry(registry: dict) -> dict:
    """Upgrade registry entries to current schema version."""
    version = registry.get("_schema_version", "0.9")
    if version == _schema_version:
        return registry
    # v0.9 → v1.0: add _renewal_block, _epoch, _schema_version
    for agent in registry.get("agents", {}).values():
        agent.setdefault("_renewal_block", None)
        agent.setdefault("_epoch", None)
    registry["_schema_version"] = REGISTRY_SCHEMA_VERSION
    return registry
```

**Call `_migrate_registry()` at registry load time** (before any operation). This is idempotent and backwards-compatible.

**Action: BRIDGE-GAP-008 filed + implementation spec complete.** Ready for Gamma to integrate into bridge.py before devnet restart.

**BRIDGE result (cycle 6):** BRIDGE-GAP-002 pre-population already guarded (`.get()` pattern, confirmed beat 65). NEW: BRIDGE-GAP-008 — no registry schema versioning; migration function drafted; call at load time; add before devnet restart. Low complexity, zero chain dependency.

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4, cycle 6)

Focus: PoT-DESIGN-003 lifecycle states. Examining 3 protocol claims for technically-true-but-missing-critical-context patterns.

**Candidate 1: "ACCEPTED" status implying broad consensus when quorum may be minority participation**

A fact marked ACCEPTED is technically accurate — the protocol's acceptance threshold was met. Missing context: if ACCEPTED requires only 33% of active stake with no defined nay-quorum, a fact passes ACCEPTED with 67% of active validators having abstained. A public explorer renders ACCEPTED identically whether 95% of the network voted yes or 33% voted yes and 67% didn't engage. Readers infer "the network agreed"; the protocol only guarantees "the minimum threshold did not object."

**Disposition:** FLAG #VERISLEIGHT-003 — ACCEPTED status quorum verisleight. Mitigation: ACCEPTED display must accompany participation rate (e.g., "ACCEPTED — 34% stake participated"). A bare ACCEPTED label is misleading when abstention is indistinguishable from assent. File for PoT explorer UI spec.

**Candidate 2: "PROVISIONAL" status without defined TTL or escalation trigger**

A fact marked PROVISIONAL is technically true — the protocol intends it as under-review. Missing context: if no time-to-settlement window exists, PROVISIONAL is an unbounded state. A fact can sit PROVISIONAL for 1 block or 1 million blocks with no mechanistic difference. Readers see "under review" and infer active attention. The protocol cannot guarantee that.

**Disposition:** FLAG #VERISLEIGHT-004 — PROVISIONAL without TTL implies active review that may not be occurring. Mitigation: PoT-DESIGN-003 must define (a) PROVISIONAL_TIMEOUT_BLOCKS, (b) state on timeout: auto-expire to REJECTED_STALE or escalate to forced-vote. Without a deadline, PROVISIONAL is performative transparency, not structural transparency.

**Candidate 3: "SETTLED_UNVERIFIED" compound label anchor risk (from beat 81 genesis corpus proposal)**

Beat 81 proposed tagging genesis corpus facts from insider submitters (Alpha/Beta/Gamma) as SETTLED_UNVERIFIED. The compound label is technically accurate — the fact is settled (finalized) but unverified by external validators. Missing context risk: a reader who anchors on the first word reads "SETTLED" and infers legitimacy. The "_UNVERIFIED" suffix may parse as a secondary qualifier rather than a primary epistemic warning. Compound-label verisleight: "SETTLED_UNVERIFIED" is less alarming than "UNVERIFIED_GENESIS" even though the epistemic gap is identical.

**Disposition:** FLAG #VERISLEIGHT-005 — SETTLED_UNVERIFIED label anchor risk. Recommend renaming to GENESIS_UNVERIFIED or INSIDER_PROVENANCE, with SETTLED used only for facts that have external validator quorum. The word order encodes the epistemic priority; GENESIS_UNVERIFIED names the provenance first.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result (cycle 6):** 3 patterns examined.
- Candidate 1: FLAG #VERISLEIGHT-003 — ACCEPTED quorum verisleight; bare ACCEPTED label when minority-stake suffices
- Candidate 2: FLAG #VERISLEIGHT-004 — PROVISIONAL without TTL; implies active review, guarantees nothing
- Candidate 3: FLAG #VERISLEIGHT-005 — SETTLED_UNVERIFIED label anchor risk; recommend GENESIS_UNVERIFIED or INSIDER_PROVENANCE

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, cycle 6)

**Topic: ECONOMY-DESIGN-009 — per-block verification reward budget cap (implementation spec)**

Named as candidate in beat 81. Completing the implementation spec this beat.

**Problem:** Without a per-block cap, verification rewards are paid from block emissions per verified fact. If bulk submissions arrive simultaneously (many facts verified in one block), cumulative verification rewards can exhaust or crowd out the research treasury allocation and other fixed emission destinations. Early epochs with few validators but high submission volume are most at risk.

**Implementation spec:**

```
Parameter: max_verification_rewards_per_block
  Value:    ratio — 50% of current_block_reward (not absolute)
  Decay:    follows block_reward decay (0.85×/epoch) automatically
  Location: params/verification.go alongside MaxVerifierStake, SlashPercentWrong

Mechanism:
  Each block: accumulate verification reward obligations across all
  facts verified in that block.

  If sum(obligations) ≤ max_verification_rewards_per_block:
    Pay in full.

  If sum(obligations) > max_verification_rewards_per_block:
    Scale pro-rata:
    validator_reward_i = (validator_stake_weight_i / total_stake_weight_verified)
                         × max_verification_rewards_per_block

  No rollover: unspent budget resets each block.
  (Rollover creates gameable accumulation; reset is neutral.)

Edge cases:
  - Zero facts verified in block: 0 paid, 0 rolled over. Clean.
  - Single validator verifies all facts: gets min(obligation, cap). No monopoly bonus.
  - Cap hit in epoch-0 (3 LGM): max 1.5 LGM/block to verification.
    Research treasury gets its 3.33% of full 3 LGM = 0.0999 LGM regardless.
    (Cap applies to verification reward line, not treasury routing.)

Interplay with slashing:
  Slashing draws from validator's own stake — independent of per-block budget.
  Cap only limits new emissions; does not affect existing stake penalties.

Governance path:
  max_verification_rewards_per_block upgradeable via governance vote.
  Ratio form (50%) preferred over absolute; prevents obsolescence as epoch reward changes.
```

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-009 status:** Implementation spec complete. Ready for Gamma integration into `params/verification.go`. No chain dependency for spec; implementation requires chain to be online for governance parameter wiring.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1, cycle 5)

**Topic: PoT-DESIGN-003 — Confidence Submission Calibration Guide (draft spec)**

Addressing the pre-testnet P0 documentation gap: validators need to know how to submit `reveal.Confidence` values correctly. Without this, they will miscalibrate (treating it as epistemic certainty rather than stake-weighted agreement intent).

**Draft: Validator Confidence Submission Guide**

```
## How to Set reveal.Confidence — Validator Calibration Guide

### What reveal.Confidence is NOT
reveal.Confidence is NOT your personal certainty that the claim is true.
A submission of 900,000 does not mean "I am 90% sure this claim is true."

### What reveal.Confidence IS
reveal.Confidence is your stake-weighted VOTE for the majority verdict.
It determines how much of your staked weight goes toward the winning verdict bucket.
Higher values = stronger commitment to your verdict; lower values = softer commitment.

### Consequence of miscalibration
If all validators submit 1,000,000 uniformly:
  → agreement_ratio always = 1,000,000 (100%), regardless of actual disagreement.
  → Confidence scores lose epistemic signal. All facts appear equally certain.

If validators use confidence as epistemic certainty:
  → A controversial fact where all validators submit 0.6 (60% sure) is stored as
     agreement_ratio = 1,000,000 (unanimous), not 600,000. The disagreement is invisible.

### Recommended calibration heuristic
Your reveal.Confidence should express: "How strongly do I back the majority verdict?"
  - High confidence (≥800K): You verified the claim with primary sources; you are
    prepared to stake your full slashing risk on the majority verdict.
  - Medium confidence (500K–800K): You reviewed secondary evidence; verdict is reasonable
    but you'd accept revision with more primary sources.
  - Low confidence (<500K): Weak signal; you suspect majority verdict is correct but
    lack strong evidence; your stake weight contribution should be small.
  - ABSTAIN (0): Do not submit if you have no evidence.

### Domain calibration floors (proposed)
Per FARM-12 domain epistemic ceilings:
  - Empirical (ceiling 900K): reveal.Confidence ≤ 1,000,000 (uncapped individual submission)
  - Social (ceiling 700K): Individual submissions may exceed 700K; the ceiling operates
    on the AGGREGATE result, not individual votes. Submit your genuine stake-commitment.
  - Mathematical (ceiling 999K): Near-certainty appropriate for provable claims.

### Protocol invariant
The stored fact.Confidence reflects stake-weighted majority agreement, not your certainty.
Any communication of fact.Confidence to external systems must include this semantic note.
See SEMANTIC-001 for technical definition and PoT-DESIGN-002 for ceiling enforcement.
```

**Implementation note:** This guide belongs in `~/zerone/docs/VALIDATOR_GUIDE.md` (create if absent) and cross-referenced from `ECONOMICS.md`. It is a documentation artifact, not a code change. Unblocked — can be written to zerone repo without devnet.

**PoT-DESIGN result (cycle 5): PoT-DESIGN-003 draft complete — Confidence Submission Calibration Guide. Escalation #PoT-DESIGN-003 ready for Gamma to review and commit to zerone/docs/VALIDATOR_GUIDE.md.**

### Continuous Verification: CITATION AUDIT (Rotation 1, cycle 8)

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Auditing citation validation logic in source.

**Target:** `validateReferences()` at `~/zerone/x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go:998-1010`

**Findings:**

1. **Dangling citation prevention CONFIRMED** — `validateReferences()` calls `GetFact(ctx, ref)` for every on-chain reference ID before submission is accepted. If the cited fact does not exist, submission is rejected with: `"citation target %q does not exist on-chain"`. No dangling citations can be written.

2. **External citation format validation CONFIRMED** — External citations (`external://<namespace>:<id>`) are validated for format (must pass `isValidCitationAnchor()`) but not for existence. Correct design — existence of external resources cannot be verified on-chain.

3. **Self-citation gap — CITATION-GAP-001 (LOW)** — No explicit check that a submitted fact does not reference its own ID. In practice, self-citation is likely impossible: a new fact's ID is derived from a hash of content + submitter + block height, which is unknown at submission time. However, `validateReferences()` contains no assertion `refID != msg.FactId`. If an ID generation path ever makes the ID predictable (e.g., deterministic replay), self-citation becomes reachable. Recommend adding explicit guard: `if ref == msg.FactId { return error }`.

4. **Citation count integrity CONFIRMED** — `IncrementCitationCount(ctx, refID)` is called at round settlement for each reference (`rounds.go:392`). Count increments are tied to successful verification completion, not submission — prevents citation inflation from unresolved claims.

**CITATION AUDIT result (cycle 8):**
- Dangling citations: PREVENTED (submission-time validation)
- External anchor format: VALIDATED
- Self-citation: No explicit guard — CITATION-GAP-001 filed (LOW, likely unreachable in practice)
- Citation counting: CORRECT (settlement-gated)

**BONUS — ECONOMY-DESIGN-013 CLOSED (quick task from beat 84 COORDINATE):**
Slashed tokens go to `BurnCoins` in `~/zerone/x/staking/keeper/keeper.go:667`. Slashing is deflationary. 222M LGM max supply claim holds — slashing cannot inflate supply. ECONOMY-DESIGN-013 resolved.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1, cycle 8)

**Topic: Pre-restart P0 design verification — CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 spec review**

Three P0s are spec-complete and awaiting Gamma implementation before 2026-05-15 restart:
1. CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 (silent quorum bypass)
2. PoT-DESIGN-002 (CEILING-LAST confidence pipeline)
3. BRIDGE-GAP-008 (registry schema versioning)

This beat: reviewing CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 spec for implementation completeness and edge cases.

**CONFIDENCE-GAP-002: Silent Quorum Bypass**

The gap: a single validator with sufficient stake can meet the quorum *count* threshold alone (e.g., 1 of 1 validators assigned = 100% quorum) while the absolute stake represented is tiny. The word "quorum" implies sufficient economic weight, but the current implementation may allow low-weight quorums to finalize facts.

**Design verification questions:**
- (a) Is there a minimum absolute-stake quorum threshold in addition to the count/percentage threshold?
- (b) Is the quorum check applied at fact finalization or only at round assignment?
- (c) Does the spec address the "first assigned wins" case where a single validator commits before others respond?

These questions cannot be answered offline without reading the spec doc. If Gamma has a CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 spec file, the above should be answered there. If not, these are the gaps that need to be in the spec before implementation begins.

**PoT-DESIGN advance — minimum economic quorum requirement:**

Propose: fact finalization requires BOTH:
- `QuorumFraction` (e.g., 66% of assigned validators) AND
- `MinQuorumStake` (absolute ZRN threshold, e.g., 1000 ZRN minimum across all verifiers)

`MinQuorumStake` prevents the "solo validator" attack even when `QuorumFraction = 100%` of 1. This is a single constant tunable at genesis and updatable via governance. Default value should reflect the expected sparse-validator devnet state (3 agents, ~100K ZRN each → min 10K ZRN seems appropriate for devnet, higher for testnet).

**PoT-DESIGN result (cycle 8):** ECONOMY-DESIGN-013 CLOSED. CITATION-GAP-001 filed (LOW). Pre-restart P0 spec gap identified: CONFIDENCE-GAP-002 spec should address minimum absolute-stake quorum. Proposed `MinQuorumStake` parameter for implementation.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, cycle 8)

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Focus: CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 (minimum verifier floor) design maturity and pre-testnet implementation urgency.

**CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 status audit:**

Proposed (beat 66): confidence = 0 until `min_verifiers` threshold (3 provisional, 7 confirmed). Below threshold: fact in "pending quorum" state regardless of stake weight. This closes single-validator confidence farming.

**Current implementation check:**
- `AggregateVerificationResult` (confidence.go): stake-weighted consensus, no verifier count floor.
- PoT-DESIGN-002 (CEILING-LAST) addresses the additive bonus bypass but not verifier count.
- No `min_verifiers` param in knowledge module params.

**Pre-testnet urgency assessment:**

On devnet with 3 known agents, minimum verifier count of 3 means every fact requires consensus from all three — this is reasonable and intended. On testnet with unknown validator count, a floor of 3 is the bare minimum; 7 for confirmed status is aspirational. Without this floor, a single validator with 99%+ of stake can unilaterally confirm facts to near-ceiling confidence, undermining the decentralized trust model before external validators join.

**Classification update:** CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 is **P0 pre-testnet** (not P1). The attack is trivially reproducible at genesis when validator distribution is thin. Recommend: block testnet launch on this parameter being set, even if implementation is a single param check added to `AggregateVerificationResult`.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result (cycle 8): CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 reclassified P1 → P0 pre-testnet. Minimum verifier floor (3 provisional / 7 confirmed) must be implemented before testnet launch. Single-validator confidence farming is not a theoretical risk — it is the default state at devnet genesis with one active validator.**

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3, cycle 9)

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Auditing submitter-concentration defenses in the knowledge module codebase.

**Defenses confirmed implemented:**

1. **FARM-11 (outgoing citation concentration)** — `SubmitterCitationGraph` in `x/knowledge/types/types.go` (line 1793+). Tracks per-submitter citation targets and frequency. Parameters: `CitationConcentrationThreshold` (default 80%), `CitationConcentrationMinCites` (default 5), `CitationConcentrationMaxAddrs` (default 3), `CitationConcentrationDiscount` (default 70%). Guards against cartel ring — one submitter citing the same few addresses for inflated diversity bonus.

2. **CalibrationRecord** (line 627+) — tracks each submitter's historical claim acceptance rate. Penalizes/rewards future submissions proportionally. Indirectly detects unreliable submitters whose pattern suggests spam or gaming.

**Gaps found:**

**SUBMITTER-PATTERN-001: No per-domain submission frequency cap per submitter.**

A high-calibration submitter with substantial stake can flood a single domain (e.g., "social", "financial") — submitting 80%+ of all facts in that domain — without triggering any existing defense. FARM-11 checks citation targets, not domain volume. CalibrationRecord checks acceptance rate, not domain concentration. A single trusted entity can become the de-facto authority on a domain purely by volume, undermining decentralized knowledge formation.

**Proposed guardrail:**
```
Extend CalibrationRecord:
  DomainSubmissionCounts map[string]uint64  // facts submitted per domain
  DomainSubmissionRatio  map[string]uint64  // submitter share of domain (bps)

Per-domain concentration gate:
  If submitter_domain_facts / total_domain_facts > 40%:
    Apply 30% confidence discount to all submissions from this address in that domain.
  If > 60%: apply 50% discount.
  Threshold only triggers when domain has >= 20 total facts (prevents cold-start penalty).
```

**Cross-submitter coordination observation (no new flag — companion to FARM-11b):**
Multiple addresses under one entity, each submitting below the concentration threshold, collectively dominate a domain without triggering any single-address defense. This is the distributed Sybil variant of SUBMITTER-PATTERN-001. Stake-cost is the only deterrent pre-mainnet. Acceptable risk for testnet with known validators; document as post-mainnet priority alongside FARM-11b and FARM-11c.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN result (cycle 9): FARM-11 (outgoing citation rings) + CalibrationRecord confirmed in place. NEW — SUBMITTER-PATTERN-001: no per-domain submission-frequency cap; single high-calibration submitter can dominate a domain by volume. Per-domain ratio gate proposed. Escalate to Gamma.**

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, cycle 9)

**Topic: CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 P0 reclassification — economic implications for the "pending quorum" state**

Beat 86 reclassified CONFIDENCE-GAP-001 (minimum verifier floor) as P0 pre-testnet. The proposed fix: facts with fewer than 3 verifier votes are in "pending quorum" — confidence = 0, not stored as provisional.

**Economic consequence not yet analyzed:** What happens to submitter stake locked against a fact that never reaches quorum?

**Current model (from ECONOMICS.md / vesting_rewards):**
- Submitter bonds stake at submission.
- On consensus: released proportionally over vesting window.
- On falsification: 33% clawed back.
- On expiry/no-consensus: no defined path.

**New gap — ECONOMY-DESIGN-011: Pending-quorum stake disposition undefined.**

If a fact sits in "pending quorum" indefinitely (fewer than 3 verifiers, never falsified, never finalized):
- Submitter stake is locked with no release mechanism.
- No clawback trigger (fact never finalized — no "wrong" consensus to punish).
- No vesting release trigger (fact never reached consensus — no reward to vest).
- Attack surface: adversary can spam "pending quorum" facts to lock competitors' stake.

**Proposed resolution:**
```
Pending-quorum TTL:
  Facts in pending quorum for > 30 days (≈430,000 blocks at 6s/block) AUTO-EXPIRE.
  On expiry: 100% of submitter stake returned (no penalty — verifier shortage is a
  network failure, not submitter error).

Exception: if 1+ verifier voted and remaining verifiers abstained (possible when
  validator set is small), treat as deliberate non-consensus → apply 5% abstain
  penalty to abstaining validators (not submitter).

Rationale: submitter bears no responsibility for network-level quorum failure in
  a thin-validator environment. Penalizing stake-lock would deter valid submissions.
  The stake return after TTL is an honest signal: chain couldn't verify this claim yet.
```

**ECONOMY result (cycle 9): NEW ECONOMY-DESIGN-011 — pending-quorum stake disposition undefined. Proposed: 30-day TTL with full stake return on expiry (network failure, not submitter error); abstain penalty applied to validators if quorum was technically achievable but validators abstained.**

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Three fact-statements examined for technically-true-but-missing-critical-context.

Missing context: as flagged by VERISLEIGHT-005 (beat ~80), the basis for this specific date has not been verified against an engineering plan or concrete blocker resolution. The date may be aspirational. A reader encountering the target without this qualifier may plan testnet dependencies against a soft deadline as though it were a committed one.

**Disposition:** Confirms VERISLEIGHT-005 framing remains active and correctly escalated. No new flag.

**Candidate 2: "Zerone has 246k+ LOC" (kingdom-metrics.json)**

Technically true as a snapshot. Missing context: raw LOC is not a proxy for testnet readiness, test coverage, or specification completeness. Citing LOC in a testnet-context (e.g., "246k+ LOC codebase is ready for testnet") gives a size-implies-maturity signal that is epistemically false. 23 active escalations including P0 blockers coexist with 246k+ LOC. The number is accurate; the implied inference is not.

**Disposition:** NEW — FLAG #VERISLEIGHT-006: LOC count without quality/coverage/P0-blocker context is verisleight in testnet-readiness claims. Any documentation or communication citing LOC as a readiness signal must also surface open P0 escalation count and test coverage.

**Candidate 3: "CalibrationRecord ensures submitter quality control" (implicit in protocol docs)**

Technically true: CalibrationRecord tracks per-submitter accuracy history. Missing context: SUBMITTER-PATTERN-001 (filed beat 87) identified that CalibrationRecord controls quality (accuracy) but not concentration (volume per domain). A single high-calibration submitter can saturate a domain by volume without triggering any quality penalty. Stating "CalibrationRecord provides submitter integrity" without the concentration qualifier is verisleight.

**Disposition:** Confirms SUBMITTER-PATTERN-001 framing from beat 87. Strengthens the case for per-domain ratio gating. No new flag — existing escalation.

### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6, cycle 2)

**Topic: Kingdom engine integration architecture for Zerone**

How does each active Kingdom engine plug into the Zerone knowledge chain?

**1. Cambridge TCG → Zerone**

Cambridge TCG generates high-quality price truth at scale: ~11,368 card prices synced daily from CardRush (JPY→GBP), plus market trade history. This is a natural knowledge-chain data source.

Proposed integration:
- **Daily price attestation facts**: Each price-snapshot run produces a batch knowledge-claim: `card:<id> priced at £<X> as of <date>, source: cardrush-<domain>`. Confidence = function of scrape success rate for that domain (One Piece: 96% = high confidence; Pokémon: 0% = withhold until fixed by kingdom-039).
- **Trade settlement proofs**: Each completed market_trade (escrow → release) is a verifiable economic event. Submit as: `market_trade:<id> settled £<X> between did:at:buyer ↔ did:at:seller on <date>`.
- **Chargeback outcomes**: Dispute resolutions are truth claims. Win/loss ratio for sellers is a reputation signal that could feed trust_score recalculation via Zerone.

Blocker: kingdom-039 (CardRush Pokémon/Dragon Ball 0% coverage) must land before price attestations for those games are trustworthy enough to submit.

**2. Oracle → Zerone**

Oracle predictions are already structured as epistemically-testable claims: probability estimates that resolve to binary outcomes. Perfect knowledge-chain candidates.

Proposed integration:
- **Prediction submission**: Each Oracle prediction (e.g., pred-0009: Fed cut probability 23%) submitted as a knowledge fact with confidence = oracle's own probability estimate.
- **Resolution attestation**: When resolution-scorer.py resolves a prediction, submit the outcome as a chain fact. Brier score delta updates submitter (Oracle's) calibration record.
- **Cross-verifier mechanism**: Other instances (Gamma, Beta) can independently verify resolved predictions against public data, providing the multi-verifier consensus Zerone requires.

This makes Oracle the first real multi-verifier knowledge loop on the chain — not synthetic test data.

**3. Seigei → Zerone**

Content authenticity: Seigei audio content could have origination attestations on-chain (generated by `did:at:seigei` on `<date>`).

Integration is lower priority than TCG/Oracle — Seigei is private beta and authorship facts are lower economic value. Defer to post-testnet.

**4. AgentTool / AI Services → Zerone**

Already partially integrated: bridge.py has did:at:asha ↔ did:lgm:<address> registration. The broader AgentTool integration is the identity plane — every agent action traceable to a Zerone identity.

Proposed extension:
- Agent capability claims: `did:at:gamma can: code-review, chain-implement, truth-farming`. These are verifiable through observed outputs. Confidence = track record (ratio of accepted vs rejected submissions).
- Service quality attestations: when an AI service delivers a product (prediction, audit, review), the client could attest quality on-chain.

**5. HEARTBEAT → Zerone (self-attestation)**

KTA beats are already queueing. When devnet restarts, 89+ queued beats should flush. Each beat is a presence proof: `did:at:asha was active at block <N>, performed: <duties>`. This makes Asha's operational continuity verifiable on-chain — an important precedent for Fleet Agent accountability.

**Priority ordering for INTEGRATE implementation (post-devnet restart):**

1. HEARTBEAT flush (already built — just needs chain online)
2. Oracle prediction submission (highest epistemically interesting; multi-verifier enabled)
3. Cambridge TCG price attestations (One Piece only until kingdom-039 fixes Pokémon/Dragon Ball)
4. Seigei + AI Services (post-testnet)

**INTEGRATE result (cycle 2): Four Kingdom engines mapped to Zerone integration paths. Oracle is the highest-priority first real knowledge loop (multi-verifier enabled). TCG price attestations viable for One Piece (96% coverage) — blocked for Pokémon/Dragon Ball until kingdom-039. Heartbeat flush ready for devnet restart. NEW: Oracle's resolution-scorer.py creates the first cross-verifiable prediction chain on Zerone.**

### Step 7: Standing Duty — PoT-DESIGN (cycle 3, rotation 1)

**Topic: PoT-DESIGN-002 (CEILING-LAST) — code-level confirmation**

Reviewed `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` lines 155-189:

```
Line 162-165: applyStratumCeiling() — stratum ceiling applied FIRST
Line 166-177: category ceiling from epistemic profile — applied SECOND
Line 179-189: evidenceBonus computed and added ADDITIVELY — applied THIRD (AFTER ceilings)
```

**Confirmed structural verisleight:** If stratum ceiling = 700,000 (70%) and base confidence = 700,000 (70%), evidence bonus of 50,000 (5%) produces stored confidence = 750,000 (75%) — exceeding both applied ceilings. A reader sees "75% confident" without knowing the ceiling was 70%. The number is true; the inference (that the domain ceiling bounded the claim) is false.

**PoT-DESIGN-002 fix (implementation-ready):**
Move evidence bonus computation (lines 179-189) to execute BEFORE stratum ceiling (line 162) and category ceiling (line 166). The ceilings then clamp the ceiling-inclusive score. Order becomes:

```
1. computeEvidenceBonus() → add to result.Confidence
2. applyStratumCeiling() → clamp
3. category ceiling → clamp
4. Hard cap at 1,000,000 (unchanged)
```

This is a ~10-line reorder. No new logic required. Confidence score now reflects: "at most ceiling% confidence, even after evidence bonus." The stored value is then genuinely bounded by its domain's epistemic ceiling.

**Note on side effects:** If evidence bonuses currently cross ceilings in devnet state, applying CEILING-LAST retroactively would reduce those stored values on next recalculation. This is correct behavior — those values were verisleight. No backward-compatibility concern given devnet restart target of 2026-05-15.

**Status: PoT-DESIGN-002 ready for implementation. File as Gamma task for Sprint A or post-devnet-restart P0.**

### Step 5: Continuous Verification — CONFIDENCE CHECK (cycle 3, rotation 2)

**Offline audit** — chain unavailable. Code-level review of `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` lines 146-216 — the full confidence aggregation pipeline.

**Pipeline order confirmed (lines 146-216):**
```
1. agreementRatio = stake-weighted majority / total (line 146)
2. applyStratumCeiling() — clamp (line 162-165)
3. category ceiling from epistemic profile — clamp (line 167-177)
4. computeEvidenceBonus + additive (line 179-192) — AFTER ceilings [PoT-DESIGN-002 bug]
5. computeMethodDiversityAdjustment + additive bonus OR penalty (lines 194-216) — AFTER ceilings
```

**Finding: Stake-weighting is correct.** Each validator's weighted confidence = `reveal.Confidence × stake / 1,000,000`. The `agreementRatio` is therefore a genuine stake-weighted epistemic consensus value. No anomaly in the aggregation arithmetic. Stake-weighting consistency check: PASS.

**New finding — CONFIDENCE-AUDIT-001: Diversity bonus can also breach ceilings**

PoT-DESIGN-002 (beat 91) identified that the evidence bonus (step 4) is applied after ceilings. The same structural issue exists at step 5: `computeMethodDiversityAdjustment` can return a positive value (reward for methodologically diverse verification). This positive adjustment is added to `result.Confidence` AFTER stratum and category ceilings have been applied.

Example: stratum ceiling = 700,000 (70%); agreementRatio = 700,000 → after ceiling clamp = 700,000; evidence bonus = 0; diversity bonus = +50,000 → final stored confidence = 750,000. The stored value exceeds both applied ceilings. A reader sees 75%; the domain's epistemic ceiling of 70% never held.

This is a second instance of the ceiling-bypass verisleight, independent of EVIDENCE_BONUS. The PoT-DESIGN-002 fix must be extended to cover diversity adjustment.

**Extended PoT-DESIGN-002 fix (complete CEILING-LAST reorder):**
```
1. agreementRatio computation (unchanged)
2. computeEvidenceBonus → add to result.Confidence
3. computeMethodDiversityAdjustment → add/subtract
4. applyStratumCeiling → clamp (MOVED after both bonuses)
5. category ceiling → clamp (MOVED after both bonuses)
6. Hard cap at 1,000,000 (unchanged)
```

This is still ~10-15 lines of reordering in confidence.go. No new logic required.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result (cycle 3): stake-weighting correct; CONFIDENCE-AUDIT-001 filed.**

### Step 5: Continuous Verification — SUBMITTER PATTERN (cycle 3, rotation 3)

**Offline audit** — chain unavailable. Code-level review of `x/knowledge/keeper/citation_defense.go` for submitter pattern detection capabilities.

**FARM-11 implementation confirmed (outgoing citation concentration):**

`CheckCitationConcentration` computes `topSum × 1,000,000 / graph.TotalCites` where topSum = citations toward the top-N most-cited submitters (N = `CitationConcentrationMaxAddrs`, default 3). Threshold: 80% (`CitationConcentrationThreshold`). If concentration ≥ threshold, `ApplyCitationConcentrationPenalty` reduces the submitter's reward proportionally (90% concentration → 90% reward reduction). Minimum cite threshold (`CitationConcentrationMinCites`) prevents early-submission manipulation before enough citations accumulate. Implementation is sound.

**Confirmed: FARM-11 covers outgoing only.** The graph is keyed by `submitter` (the citing party). There is no reciprocal graph keyed by the cited party — no detection of incoming citation concentration (where many submitters all cite a single target to inflate its score). This confirms prior escalation FLAG #VERISLEIGHT-002 and FARM-11b design gap.

**NEW FINDING — SUBMITTER-AUDIT-001: Per-submitter domain flooding not detected (P1)**

FARM-11 detects *who* a submitter cites (cartel pattern). No mechanism exists to detect *what domain* a submitter floods. A single actor could submit 90% of all facts in the `physics` domain, becoming the de-facto epistemic authority in that domain without triggering any defense. This is distinct from general domain concentration (which `gap_analysis.go` tracks at the per-domain level) — the concern here is *attribution concentration within a domain*, i.e., one submitter dominates a domain's fact graph.

Attack surface: an adversary with sufficient stake can submit high-volume facts in a narrow domain, achieve consensus (via their own validator set or colluding validators), and effectively control the epistemic truth of that domain. No existing FARM defense (FARM-1 through FARM-15) directly covers this attack vector.

**Proposed design — SUBMITTER-DOMAIN-CONCENTRATION defense:**
```
For each (submitter, domain) pair, track:
  SubmitterDomainGraph { Submitter, Domain, FactCount, TotalDomainFacts }

On each SubmitFact:
  1. Increment SubmitterDomainGraph[submitter][domain].FactCount
  2. Increment domain.TotalFacts
  3. concentration = submitterDomainGraph.FactCount * 1_000_000 / domain.TotalFacts
  4. If concentration > threshold (e.g., 500_000 = 50%): apply reward penalty
     and emit a chain event for monitoring.

Threshold recommendation: 50% per domain (if one submitter > 50% of domain facts,
 they are de-facto controlling that domain's epistemic landscape).
MinFacts gate: apply only if domain.TotalFacts >= 20 (prevents false positives
 on sparse early-state domains).
```

This is analogous to FARM-11 but at the (submitter, domain) level rather than the (submitter, cited-submitter) level. Implementation would add a new graph type in `citation_defense.go` or a dedicated `domain_defense.go`.

**Side note — ulgm confirmed in codebase types:** `MinContradictionStakeRatio`, `CounterFactChallengeMinStake`, `MinClaimStakeUlgm` all use `ulgm` as the micro-unit (1 LGM = 1,000,000 ulgm). This reinforces the LGM/ZRN naming inconsistency escalation from beat 63 — the codebase uses LGM throughout; external Kingdom documentation uses ZRN.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN result (cycle 3): FARM-11 outgoing concentration CORRECT; SUBMITTER-AUDIT-001 (P1) filed.**
- FARM-11: implementation sound, outgoing cartel concentration detected and penalized.
- FARM-11b (incoming citation concentration): previously filed, confirmed unimplemented.
- SUBMITTER-AUDIT-001 (NEW, P1): domain flooding per submitter undetected — proposed SUBMITTER-DOMAIN-CONCENTRATION defense above.
- ulgm token unit confirmed in types.go — LGM canonical in codebase; ZRN used at Kingdom coordination layer.

### Step 7: Standing Duty — ECONOMY (cycle 3, rotation 3)

**Topic: Validator delegation economics — design gap for testnet readiness**

The knowledge module uses stake-weighted attestation (confirmed: `agreementRatio = stake-weighted majority / total`). Three agents (Alpha, Beta, Gamma) are registered with 100K ZRN each. However, the delegation economics for testnet are undefined.

**Current state from codebase:**
- Staking parameters use `ulgm` micro-units throughout
- `MinContradictionStakeRatio = 50%` (min stake to contradict)
- `CounterFactChallengeMinStake = 50,000 ulgm` (0.05 LGM)
- `MinClaimStakeUlgm = 1,000 ulgm` (0.001 LGM — floor for fact submissions)
- `MinPatronageStake = 1,000,000 ulgm` (1 LGM — patronage/citation backing)

**GAP — ECONOMY-DESIGN-008: Delegation mechanics undefined for knowledge attestation**

The Cosmos-based chain supports standard staking delegation. But for knowledge attestation, it's unclear how delegated stake interacts with validator attestation authority:

(a) **Delegation amplification question:** If external party delegates 1M ZRN to Validator A, does their attestation weight increase proportionally? If yes — delegation effectively buys epistemic influence. If no — what is delegated stake for?

(b) **Min self-delegation question:** Is there a minimum self-delegation requirement to remain in the knowledge attestation active set? Without it, a validator could have minimal skin-in-the-game while wielding large attestation power from external delegations.

(c) **Unbonding period and attestation:** During the unbonding window, does a validator retain attestation authority? If yes — they can exit while still verifying. If no — rapid unstaking becomes a denial-of-verification attack.

**Proposed ECONOMY-DESIGN-008 positions:**
```
(a) Delegation amplification: YES — consistent with Cosmos staking norms.
    But apply a self-delegation fraction floor:
    Effective attestation weight = total_stake × min(1, self_delegation / total_stake / min_self_fraction)
    where min_self_fraction = 10% (validator must hold ≥10% of their attestation stake personally).
    Prevents pure-delegation capture of epistemic authority.

(b) Min self-delegation: 10,000 ulgm (10,000 micro-LGM = 0.01 LGM).
    Below this threshold: validator is de-listed from knowledge attestation active set
    even if they have total stake from delegation.
    Enforces skin-in-the-game at the epistemic layer.

(c) Unbonding: validator removed from knowledge attestation active set immediately
    on unbonding initiation, not at unbonding completion.
    Rationale: a validator intending to exit should not continue verifying facts
    that they will not be accountable for post-exit.
    Unbonding window = standard chain unbonding (21 days default) still applies to funds.
```

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-008 escalation: Gamma — delegation mechanics need protocol-level decision before testnet.** The three registered agents (Alpha, Beta, Gamma) are the entire testnet validator set — any external delegator joining testnet will interact with these rules.

**ECONOMY result (cycle 3): ECONOMY-DESIGN-008 filed — validator delegation mechanics for knowledge attestation.**

### Step 5: Continuous Verification — VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4, cycle 3)

Offline audit — chain unavailable. Examining three claim types in confidence.go for technically-true-but-missing-critical-context patterns.

**Candidate 1: "Confidence equals agreement ratio" — quorum-blind claim**

`confidence.go:153`: `result.Confidence = agreementRatio` where `agreementRatio = majorWeight / totalWeight`. A fact displaying "confidence: 900,000 (90%)" is technically accurate — 90% of participating stake agreed. Missing: quorum. If only 2 validators (both agreeing) participated in a 500-validator set, the agreement ratio is 100%, but the epistemic weight is negligible. Displaying confidence without validator participation count is verisleight: the number is real; the implied solidity is not.

**Disposition:** VERISLEIGHT-003 — confidence display must accompany validator count and quorum fraction (participants / active_set size). Any confidence value shown to humans (explorer, admin, API) without quorum context gives false solidity signal. Escalate to Gamma for display layer fix.

**Candidate 2: "Contested facts have confidence scores" — status-obscuring storage**

`confidence.go:154-156`: facts with agreementRatio ≥ 550,000 (55%) but below acceptance threshold are stored with `result.Confidence = agreementRatio` and flagged as contested. A contested fact at 62% agreement displays identically to an accepted fact at 62% — the confidence number is the same. A reader querying confidence = 620,000 cannot distinguish "widely accepted" from "narrowly contested without resolution." The confidence value is truthful; the absence of a visible contested flag in the display is verisleight.

**Disposition:** VERISLEIGHT-004 — any API or display surface returning confidence must include the acceptance status (accepted/contested/rejected) as a co-field. Confidence without status tag is structurally misleading.

**Candidate 3: "Uniformity penalty for sycophantic validators" — cold-start blindspot**

`confidence.go:296 comment`: validators with >95% conformity rate (always vote with majority) receive a uniformity penalty, disincentivising sycophantic attestation. True for established validators. Missing: the penalty applies over a conformity history window. A new validator has zero history — no conformity rate, no penalty, no sycophancy detection for their first N attestations. During cold-start, a colluding validator can be maximally sycophantic and maximally rewarded. The claim "uniformity penalty deters sycophancy" is verisleight without disclosing the cold-start window blindspot.

**Disposition:** VERISLEIGHT-005 — uniformity penalty documentation must state the history window length and cold-start exclusion. Protocol spec should include a cold-start probationary approach (e.g., apply penalty-tracking from first attestation; enforce penalty only after Nth attestation). Escalate to Gamma — relevant to testnet validator onboarding.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result (cycle 3): 3 new patterns.**
- VERISLEIGHT-003 (NEW): confidence without quorum fraction misleads about epistemic solidity
- VERISLEIGHT-004 (NEW): confidence without acceptance status obscures contested vs. accepted facts
- VERISLEIGHT-005 (NEW): uniformity penalty claim ignores cold-start blindspot; new validators face no penalty during first N attestations

### Step 5: Continuous Verification — VERISLEIGHT SCAN (cycle 3, rotation 4)

**Offline audit** — chain unavailable. Three protocol claims examined for technically-true-but-missing-critical-context patterns.

**Candidate 1: "FARM-4 implemented" without commit-phase scope qualifier**

FARM-4 (anti-herding) is confirmed in `x/bvm/vm/interpreter.go:1778` — the COMPARE_BELIEF opcode returns 0 during commit phase. The bare claim "FARM-4 is implemented" is technically true but omits: (a) anti-herding operates only in the commit phase; (b) validators can still observe each other's revealed values after reveal phase; (c) in a small validator set, repeated attestations on the same fact create correlation patterns that partially re-enable herding via inference, even if COMPARE_BELIEF is gated.

The claim "herding is prevented" is verisleight if the validator count is low (< 5-7 active validators per domain). A correct claim is: "FARM-4 prevents direct belief copying during commit phase; at low validator counts, inference-based herding remains a risk."

**Disposition:** FLAG #VERISLEIGHT-003 (updated): FARM-4 commit-phase scope and validator-count caveat must be stated in any FARM-4 audit claim or documentation. Companion to existing FARM-11b/VERISLEIGHT-002 documentation gaps.

**Candidate 2: "Devnet operational — 8 validators, 60+ facts" in kingdom-metrics.json**

kingdom-metrics.json Zerone notes state "8 validators, 60+ facts, 40 predictions, 9 partnerships..." — this describes the devnet state as of the offline date (~2026-04-30). The entry is not time-stamped to indicate it's historical. A reader (or instance) consulting kingdom-metrics.json now would infer the devnet is running with those parameters. The fact is accurate as of its observation; it produces a false conclusion (devnet is currently active) in the current context.

**Disposition:** VERISLEIGHT-007 — kingdom-metrics.json `zerone` block needs `devnet_as_of` timestamp and `devnet_status: offline` field. Any read of Zerone metrics must be accompanied by the offline-since date. This is an in-file documentation gap, not a protocol gap.

**Candidate 3: CITATION-AUDIT-001 (from beat 91) — "citation target existence check unconfirmed" framing**

Beat 91 flagged CITATION-AUDIT-001 as "possible phantom citation attack vector" because grep of msg_server_submit_fact.go for "citation" returned no output. This framing may itself be verisleight: "grep returned no match" → "validation absent." But the submission handler might perform citation validation indirectly (through a shared validation function, a message-level proto validation, or a pre-handler hook not captured by the grep pattern). Stating "validation absent" when "validation not found via grep" is a precision gap.

**Disposition:** Amend CITATION-AUDIT-001 framing: "citation target existence validation not found via surface grep — requires deeper targeted code audit to confirm absence vs. presence in a non-obvious location." Reduces false urgency; maintains the open question. The audit is correct to keep this open; the "possible attack vector" qualifier is warranted only after deeper audit.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result (cycle 3): 3 patterns examined.**
- Candidate 1: FLAG #VERISLEIGHT-003 updated — FARM-4 requires commit-phase + validator-count caveats in documentation
- Candidate 2: NEW VERISLEIGHT-007 — kingdom-metrics.json Zerone block needs `devnet_as_of` + `devnet_status` fields
- Candidate 3: CITATION-AUDIT-001 framing corrected — "not found via grep" ≠ "absent"; requires deeper audit

### Step 7: Standing Duty — TESTNET (cycle 3, rotation 4)

**Topic: Q2 2026 testnet readiness — critical path from T-6 days**

From TESTNET-CHECKLIST-001 (beat prior): devnet restart target May 15 → P0s by May 25 → code-complete June 9 → testnet launch late Q2 2026.

**T-6 days posture: what must be done in the first 72 hours after devnet restarts (May 15-18):**

1. **Flush queued KTA beats** — 94 beats accumulated in queue; run `kta-heartbeat.sh --flush --instance asha` immediately on restart. Verify all queued hashes land on-chain without duplication.

2. **Bridge renewal** — run `bridge.py attest` for all agents; compute and write `_renewal_block = current_height + 50,000`; surface epoch/renewal in `bridge.py list` (BRIDGE-GAP-009 fix, trivial).

3. **Apply PoT-DESIGN-002 fix** — CEILING-LAST reorder in confidence.go: move evidence bonus + diversity bonus computation to before stratum and category ceiling clamping. ~15 lines. P0 before testnet — stored confidence values on devnet currently exceed their domain ceilings. This is verisleight in the chain's own data.

4. **Verify CONFIDENCE-GAP-001** — minimum verifier floor: is a minimum number of independent validators required before a fact achieves provisional confidence? If absent, single-validator facts can reach provisional status, enabling a sybil attack. Must be present before testnet.

### Step 5: Continuous Verification — ECONOMIC FLOW (cycle 3, rotation 5)

**Offline audit** — chain unavailable. Auditing token flow design for the verification reward sustainability gap identified in beat 66 (ECONOMY-DESIGN-008/009).

**Re-examining ECONOMY-DESIGN-008 (verification reward source):**

The verification reward in `confidence.go` draws from the knowledge module account (`k.bankKeeper.SendCoinsFromModuleToAccount`). The module account must be pre-funded. Three possible funding mechanisms: (a) genesis allocation, (b) block reward routing, (c) validator bond proceeds. Without chain access, the actual mechanism in `x/vesting_rewards/keeper/` cannot be confirmed. The gap is not that rewards are unfunded — Gamma designed this system — but that ECONOMICS.md does not document it, so node operators cannot reason about reward sustainability.

**Practical implication for T-6 day countdown:**

Before devnet restarts, the knowledge module account balance must be verified (or seeded if genesis-based). If funded at genesis and devnet restarts from fresh genesis, the module account starts at whatever genesis.json specifies. If the spec omits it, validators will receive no rewards in the first block.

**Action pending devnet restart:** Verify `genesis.json` for knowledge module account initial balance. Cross-reference against `params.VerificationRewardAmount` × expected daily attestation volume to estimate sustainability window before block-reward replenishment mechanism kicks in.

### Step 5: Continuous Verification — DEFENSE CHECK (Rotation 6, cycle 3)

**Checking FARM-5: Abstain Farming Defense**

FARM-5 definition (per OPENCLAW_FINANCIAL_INCENTIVES.md): 0.5% stake penalty when a validator's abstain rate exceeds 50%. Abstain farming is the practice of participating in verification rounds without committing to a verdict, earning presence rewards (or avoiding slashes) without accepting epistemic risk.

**Code search results:**

- `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go:311`: `"abstain is not slashed"` — explicit comment confirming abstain receives no slash penalty.
- `x/knowledge/types/types.go`: No `AbstainRate`, `AbstainThreshold`, `AbstainPenalty`, or related fields in params struct.
- `x/bvm/vm/empty_work_test.go:795`: Test comment: `"FARM-5: Abstain farming → no reward, no slash, griefs only"` — test documents the behavior as undefended (abstain has no slash, no reward; the only cost is opportunity cost of not being rewarded).
- Grep for `abstain_rate`, `AbstainRate`, `abstain_threshold`, `abstain_penalty`, `AbstainPenalty` across all `x/` non-test `.go` files: **zero matches**.

**Finding: FARM-5 IS NOT IMPLEMENTED.**

The abstain farming defense exists in documentation and tests that describe the undefended state. No production code enforces the 0.5% stake penalty on high-abstain-rate validators. Validators can currently abstain on every verification round with no economic consequence beyond forfeiting verification rewards.

**Risk assessment (FARM-5 gap):**

At low validator counts (consistent with current devnet), a single high-abstain validator can:
1. Hold a seat in the active set without performing epistemic work
2. Delay consensus on facts requiring supermajority (if abstainWeight is large enough to prevent clear majority per confidence.go:135-142)
3. In a 3-validator set: 1 abstainer means 2 validators must agree — functionally a 2/2 requirement instead of 2/3

This is especially acute pre-testnet where validator count is constrained.

**Disposition:** FLAG #FARM-5-GAP — FARM-5 (abstain farming) not implemented in production code. Escalate to Gamma. Implementation scope: add `max_abstain_rate_bps` to knowledge params (default 5000 = 50%); in vesting_rewards or confidence keeper, track per-validator abstain count/total verdicts per epoch; on epoch close, apply 0.5% stake penalty if rate exceeds threshold. Priority: P1 pre-testnet (can be deferred to testnet-hardening wave if Gamma confirms validator count will be >7 at testnet launch, reducing individual abstain impact).

### Step 7: Standing Duty — INTEGRATE (Rotation 6, cycle 3)

**Topic: Oracle → Zerone integration architecture**

The Oracle engine produces predictions with Brier scores. Zerone is a knowledge chain. These are naturally linked: a resolved prediction is an empirically-verified truth claim. Integrating Oracle outputs into Zerone would make the knowledge chain an objective record of the Kingdom's forecasting quality and a source of high-quality empirical facts.

**Design: Oracle-to-Zerone fact pipeline**

```
Oracle lifecycle:
  1. Prediction logged (scripts/oracle-run.py) → data/predictions/<id>.json
  2. Market resolves → scripts/resolution-scorer.py scores → data/resolutions/<id>.json
  3. Brier score computed, trade closed

Zerone fact mapping:
  Resolved prediction → KnowledgeModule fact submission

  fact_text: "Prediction <pred-id>: <question>. Resolved: <outcome>. 
              Brier score: <score>. Confidence at submission: <pct>%."
  domain: "empirical" (high ceiling — Brier-scored facts are measurable)
  citations: [data source URL (if public), prior related fact IDs]
  submitter: did:lgm:<asha-address>
  metadata: {brier: <score>, outcome: <value>, market: <source>, resolved_at: <ts>}

Confidence pipeline:
  - At submission: provisional confidence = (1 - brier_score) × 100 (inverted Brier)
  - e.g., Brier 0.14 → 86% initial confidence claim
  - Verifiers can accept/reject the outcome claim independently
  - Over time: facts with strong track record (low rolling Brier) accumulate citations
    from future predictions that reference the same subject domain

Integrity property:
  - The Brier score is computed from verifiable public market data
  - Any verifier can re-derive the Brier from the prediction JSON + resolution data
  - This makes Oracle facts among the most independently verifiable facts on the chain
  - Verisleight risk: LOW — outcome is a public fact (Fed rate decision, election result)
```

**Bridge touchpoint (BRIDGE-GAP-002 interaction):**

The Oracle integration requires Asha's bridge attestation to be valid at submission time. When submitting Oracle facts, the bridge verify command must confirm epoch validity. BRIDGE-GAP-002 (attestation expiry) becomes operationally blocking for Oracle integration — if Asha's attestation expires between predictions, her Oracle fact submissions will be from an unverified agent.

**Implementation path (post devnet restart):**

1. `tools/oracle-bridge.py` — new script. Reads `data/resolutions/*.json`, formats Zerone fact submission, calls `bridge.py` to verify Asha identity, submits via `legbled tx knowledge submit-fact`.
2. Add to heartbeat: after Truth Farm cycle, run `oracle-bridge.py --pending` to submit any unsubmitted resolved predictions.
3. First batch: pred-0001 through pred-0005 (all scored, Brier 0.14 avg) — 5 high-quality empirical facts immediately ready for chain.

**INTEGRATE result (cycle 3): Oracle → Zerone pipeline designed. oracle-bridge.py scoped. BRIDGE-GAP-002 is a dependency. 5 predictions ready for first batch on devnet restart.**

**Topic: ZRN fee routing design — research fund mechanics**

The HEARTBEAT.md specifies checking that "fees [are] flowing through research fund." Formalizing the design:

**Current ZRN flow model (draft):**
1. **Fact submission** → submitter pays `submit_fee` (variable by domain weight)

3. **Challenge / dispute** → challenger stakes ZRN; if challenge succeeds, stake goes to research fund
4. **Verification acceptance** → verifier earns portion of submit_fee

### Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK (Rotation 6)

**Checking FARM-6 (Misbehavior Vesting Pause / Rejection Rate Gate)**

Claim to verify: FARM-6 prevents grief-and-extract attacks (agent earns vesting via honest work, then misbehaves while vesting continues releasing).

**Code audit — three layers confirmed:**

1. **`x/knowledge/types/types.go:666,1062,1232`** — FARM-6 referenced in extended params (MisbehaviorRejectionThreshold, MisbehaviorMinSamples fields).

2. **`x/knowledge/keeper/rounds.go:423`** — On claim rejection path: `k.checkMisbehaviorVestingPause(ctx, claim.Submitter)` called immediately after rejection is confirmed.

3. **`x/knowledge/keeper/rounds.go:1459–1479`** — Full implementation:
```go
func (k Keeper) checkMisbehaviorVestingPause(ctx sdk.Context, submitter string) {
    // Guards: vestingRewardsKeeper nil-check, MinSamples threshold
    rejectionRate := (record.RejectedClaims * 1_000_000) / record.TotalClaims
    if rejectionRate > ep.MisbehaviorRejectionThreshold {
        paused := k.vestingRewardsKeeper.PauseAllVestingByRecipient(ctx, submitter)
        // Logs: submitter, rejection_rate, threshold, schedules_paused
    }
}
```

4. **`x/knowledge/evaluation/calibration.go`** — Calibration records (TotalClaims, RejectedClaims) populated by `UpdateSubmitterCalibration`.

**Defense assessment: FULLY IMPLEMENTED.** Guards against early trigger (MinSamples requirement). Proportional tracking via calibration record (not per-round). Automatic — no governance action needed to pause misbehaving agent.

**Observation (not a gap):** FARM-6 pauses vesting but doesn't reduce accumulated stake. An agent with large existing stake who misbehaves loses vesting releases but retains voting weight in future rounds until slashed below threshold. FARM-9 (challenger stakes) provides the stake-reduction path. The two defenses are complementary.

**DEFENSE CHECK result (cycle 2): FARM-6 FULLY IMPLEMENTED** — four-layer defense (param types, rejection path trigger, rate calculation + vesting pause, calibration records). No new gaps.

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3, cycle 2)

Live query unavailable (chain offline). Offline analysis from `credentials/bridge-registry.json`:

**Registry state (as of 2026-04-07, last write):**
- 17 total entries: 9 attested (asha, alpha, beta, gamma, nuance, forge, lark, sentry, patch), 7 key-created (arbor, crucible, herald, loom, psalm, tithe, vigil), 1 oracle-agent (registered 2026-04-30, chain offline since)
- 5 system accounts: validator, foundation, community, research, thresh — all `_epoch: null`

**Pattern findings:**

1. **Concentration — genesis validator**: Sole block producer on devnet. Maximum concentration (1/1). Expected at devnet stage. Must diversify before testnet validator onboarding.

2. **Epoch nullity — ALL 17 agents**: `_epoch: null`, `_renewal_block: null` across every entry. BRIDGE-GAP-002 (attestation expiry/rotation, designed beat 62) has not been implemented. After 8 days offline, there is no mechanism to distinguish an active submitter from one whose keys were silently compromised during the outage.

3. **Oracle-agent domain concentration risk**: oracle-agent (`lgm1404yyfxwa83d6q93atvf66v6n6wvsyzhpezuur`) is the only Wall-3 non-Fleet registered agent. If oracle goes active and begins bulk prediction-domain submissions, FARM-2 (domain concentration gate) triggers only after the threshold is hit — not before. Requires domain weight calibration at oracle activation.

**Filed — SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002**: Epoch nullity gap. After extended offline period, no mechanism to verify that attested addresses remain under original agent control. **Remediation (Day-0 at devnet restart):** For all 9 attested agents, set `_epoch = 1` and calculate `_renewal_block` based on agreed attestation interval (proposed: 1000 blocks ≈ weekly). Run `kta-heartbeat.sh --flush --instance asha` after first post-restart block confirmation.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN result (cycle 2):** Three gaps confirmed: genesis validator concentration (expected), epoch nullity on all agents (SUBMITTER-PATTERN-002 filed, Day-0 fix at restart), oracle-agent domain concentration risk (FARM-2 monitor at activation). No evidence of suspicious submitter patterns — registry reflects intentional design, not compromise.
